# AES — Handover

This is the live current-state doc for the **AirlineSim Enhancement Suite (AES)** Chrome extension. A fresh session should be able to read this top-to-bottom and pick up cleanly. For long-form reference (algorithms, formulas, derivations), see `MANUAL.md`.

- **Project:** AirlineSim Enhancement Suite (AES) — Chrome MV3 extension, vanilla JS + jQuery (no build step).
- **What it is:** quality-of-life toolkit for AirlineSim, the browser airline-management game. Reads game pages via content scripts, scrapes data into `chrome.storage.local`, surfaces panels with scoring/filtering/automation. Read-mostly today; no writes back to AS.
- **Repo root:** `/Users/jihwan/Downloads/AES.v0.6.9-beta/`
- **Manifest version:** 0.6.11-beta (`manifest.json` → `version_name`).
- **Upstream author:** Zoe Bijl (https://github.com/ZoeBijl/airlinesim-enhancement-suite/). User is maintaining/extending a fork.
- **Git user:** `makeagoodusername`.

---

## 1 · Current state

### H slice 3b.2.2 — interline cost sensitivity surfacing — shipped this session
**Surfaces in:** the per-flight breakdown card ("Interline (forgone)" line in amber, between cargo revenue and fuel cost), the wave-overlay pill tooltip ("Forgone revenue ≈ $X/wk"), and the per-route popover footer (same dollar figure under the partner-count line). **Files:** `modules/route-assistant/profit-estimator.js`, `modules/route-assistant/aggregator.js` (no changes — breakdown flows through automatically), `modules/route-assistant/panel.js`, `modules/route-assistant/wave-overlay.js`.

The data through 3b.2.1 was correct and live but the *cost* of each codeshare deal was buried in the math. Users could see post-interline LF + post-interline revenue per class, but no single number answered "what is this codeshare deal taking from me each week?" — they'd have had to mentally compute `preInterlineRevenue × interlineSharePercent` from the breakdown to find out. This slice lifts that figure into three places where users naturally ask the question.

**Estimator (`profit-estimator.js`).** New breakdown fields computed alongside the existing revenue line:
- `paxRevenuePreInterline`, `cargoRevenuePreInterline`, `revenuePreInterline` — what the route would earn at the demand-derived LF if the codeshare were ended.
- `interlineRevenueLossPerFlight` = `revenuePreInterline − revenue`. Cost is fixed regardless of codeshare share, so revenue loss == profit loss; we don't need a separate profit-delta field.
- `interlineRevenueLossPerWeek` — same multiplied by frequency.

Computed inline with the existing revenue calc (same formula, just substituting `paxLoadFactorPreInterline` for the post-interline LF) so the math stays co-located and consumers can't drift from the source. Cost is unchanged by codeshare so we don't need a separate `profitPerFlightPreInterline` — `interlineRevenueLossPerFlight` IS the profit delta.

**Per-flight breakdown card (`panel.js`).** Adds an amber "Interline (forgone)" row right after the cargo revenue line (so it reads as a revenue-side adjustment, not a cost). Only renders when `interlineRevenueLossPerFlight > 0`, so non-codeshared routes look exactly as before. Color `#fbbf24` matches the wave-overlay pill stroke for cross-surface consistency.

**Wave-overlay tooltip (`wave-overlay.js`).** `_formatInterlineShareTooltip` now appends a blank line + "Forgone revenue ≈ $XK/wk" + "(cost stays the same — make sure the deal is worth it)" when the panel's lookup decorated the share with non-zero figures. New `_formatMoneyShort` helper picks compact units ($X / $XK / $X.XM) so the tooltip stays readable at typical wave-overlay zoom. The lookup itself (`panel._interlineShareLookup`) builds a one-shot dest→row map at buildSchedule time and decorates the share record with `revenueLossPerFlight` + `revenueLossPerWeek` from the matching row's profit breakdown — captured by reference so subsequent applyFleetContext calls still flow through to the tooltip without rebuilding.

**Popover footer (`panel.js`).** Right under the partner-count + per-class share summary, when the matching row has a non-zero loss, an amber "Forgone revenue ≈ $X/wk (cost stays the same)" line appears. Reads `this.rows.find(r => r.destIata === destU).profitBreakdown` — only populated when the user has a fleet/aircraft selected (estimator hasn't run otherwise), which is the same gate the breakdown card already uses, so the popover surface is consistent with the table surface.

**Why "forgone" not "cost".** The codeshare doesn't cost cash — it gives partners a fraction of the seats you'd otherwise sell. Calling it "forgone revenue" with a clarifying parenthetical ("cost stays the same — make sure the deal is worth it") makes the asymmetry explicit: this is income you're trading away, not a fee you're paying. The breakdown card uses "(forgone)" suffix; the tooltip + footer use a longer parenthetical.

**Verification.** `node --check` clean. Manual smoke: select a fleet aircraft so the estimator runs; on a route with no record the breakdown shows the existing pax/cargo/cost lines and no interline row; add a Y 30% partner via the popover → breakdown gains "Interline (forgone) −$X" between cargo revenue and fuel cost; popover footer gains "Forgone revenue ≈ $Y/wk"; wave-overlay pill tooltip ends with the same figure. Numbers cross-check: `interlineRevenueLossPerFlight × frequency ≈ interlineRevenueLossPerWeek`.

**Composability.** The new breakdown fields ride on the same row that the strategy outcomes module (`modules/strategy/outcomes.js`) already reads for fleet-level rollups. Phase 4 attribution (when the strategy module starts attributing closed-loop deltas to specific moves) can subtract `interlineRevenueLossPerWeek` from the headline P&L to separate "codeshare-induced revenue mix change" from "operational moves we made." No coupling cost today — purely a future-readiness benefit.

### H slice 3b.2.1 — interline accuracy + actionability follow-up — shipped this session
**Surfaces in:** Route Assistant service-projection (per-class revenue is now class-asymmetric-correct), wave-overlay pill (clickable + multi-line tooltip), and live consistency on popover edits (no stale pills, no stale LFs). **Files:** `modules/route-assistant/aggregator.js`, `modules/route-assistant/panel.js`, `modules/route-assistant/wave-overlay.js`. **No new storage keys, no new bus events, no manifest change.**

Three focused improvements on top of the H 3b.2 baseline (which got the data flowing through the math but treated all pax classes uniformly, made the pill purely decorative, and stopped at the `bulkLoad` call so popover edits left the panel cache stale):

**1 · Per-class accuracy in service-projection.** The H 3b.2 estimator collapses every pax-side codeshare into a single `paxPercent` and trims aggregate `paxLoadFactor` uniformly. That's the right call for the aggregate $/flt but it under-models C + F revenue when the codeshare is class-asymmetric — e.g. 30% Y interlined produces a 30% reduction in C and F revenue too, which is wrong: the partner doesn't carry C/F if the popover entry is `productClass="Y"`. Fix: `_interlineSharesFromRecord` now also returns `byClass: {Y, C, F}` where each class sums its own partner shares plus the umbrella `PAX` share. The service-projection reads the breakdown's pre-interline LF (already surfaced by H 3b.2) and applies the per-class reduction, so Y / C / F revenue and `seatsFilled` are now class-correct. Each `classBreakdown.classes[cls]` block surfaces the per-class effective LF + interline percent for the tooltip.

**2 · Pill click-through + per-class tooltip.** `_renderConnectionsOverlay` now accepts `opts.onInterlinePillClick`. When supplied (panel always supplies it), the pill `<rect>` + `<text>` get `pointer-events: auto` + a click handler that calls back into the panel. The panel's handler opens `_openInterlinePopover({destIata}, anchorEl)` when the pill's hub equals the panel's hub (the popover is hub-pinned by design); cross-hub views fall back to opening the destination's `/app/com/scheduling/<HUB><DEST>` page in a new tab. Both pill nodes also carry an SVG `<title>` element with a multi-line tooltip rendered by the new `_formatInterlineShareTooltip` — surfaces the per-class breakdown when `byClass` is populated, falls back to the aggregate `Pax: N% interlined` when only umbrella PAX entries exist, and ends with "Click to edit partners." so the action is discoverable. Pills now have `data-aes-dest` so future hover wiring can target a specific destination without re-walking the connection list.

**3 · Live cache invalidation on save.** New `_onInterlineRecordSaved(hubIata, destIata, record)` hook on the panel: updates `this.interlineByPair`, walks `this.rows` to update the matching row's `interlineShares`, runs `applyFleetContext` to re-derive estimator outputs, busts `this._waveBuild`, and triggers `this._render()`. Wired into the three popover save sites (`removePartner`, `addPartner`, `clear`) and into the settings-expander bulk clear (which uses a single batch invalidation rather than one call per cleared pair to avoid N renders). After this slice the data flow is closed-loop: edit a partner → the wave-overlay pill updates immediately, the per-class revenue split updates immediately, and the row's $/flt picks up the new LF reduction without waiting for a hub re-mount.

**Verification.** `node --check` clean across all three modified files. Manual smoke: open the popover on a route with no record → add a partner with `productClass="Y"`, share 30% → pill appears on the wave-overlay curve with "Y 30%" label and a tooltip listing "• Y: 30% interlined"; click the pill → popover re-opens for the same route; service-config tooltip shows the C and F class revenue unchanged while Y is reduced ~24% (≈ 0.3 × Y revenue share). Bulk-clear from the settings expander → all pills disappear and all $/flt revert to pre-interline values without page reload. Routes with no record render exactly as before — bit-for-bit on the pre-3b.2 baseline.

**v1 deferrals.** No changes from H 3b.2's deferrals (cross-hub cache hydration, explicit class-by-class breakdown in the pill label). The pill label still uses the compact aggregate format ("Y 30%" / "C 30%" / "P 30% · C 30%") because the tooltip is now the source of class-level detail, and a longer pill label would crowd the bezier midpoint at typical wave-overlay zoom.

### H slice 3b.2 — wave-overlay + estimator interline-share integration — shipped this session
**Surfaces in:** Route Assistant wave-overlay (a small "Y 30%" / "P 30% · C 30%" pill on every interline-classified connection curve where a per-route record carries share) + every profit estimate rendered by the panel (effective LF reduced by the recorded codeshare percent). **Files:** `modules/route-assistant/panel.js` (cache field + `refresh()` bulk-load + buildSchedule ctx wiring + new `_interlineShareLookup()` method), `modules/route-assistant/aggregator.js` (`buildRouteRows` ingestion + per-row `interlineShares` field + new `_interlineSharesFromRecord` / `_lookupInterlineRecord` helpers), `modules/route-assistant/profit-estimator.js` (new `interlineShares` input + post-source LF trim + breakdown surfacing of pre/post values), `modules/route-assistant/wave-overlay.js` (`buildSchedule` annotates connections with `interlineShare` via `ctx.interlineShareLookup`; `_renderConnectionsOverlay` paints SVG `<rect>` + `<text>` pill at curve midpoint; new `_formatInterlineShareLabel` helper).

Closes the H 3b.2 follow-up planned alongside the bulk view (3b.1.1) and the per-route popover (3b.1): the operational layer of partner-share data was being captured, audited, and bulk-listed — but never *consumed*. With this slice, the same record that the user typed into the popover now reduces effective LF in the estimator's pax + cargo math AND surfaces visually on the wave overlay so the codeshare share is legible from the at-a-glance view, not just the popover modal.

**Estimator math (additive).** New input `interlineShares: {paxPercent, cargoPercent}`, both clamped to [0, 100], default 0. Reduction is applied AFTER LF source attribution (override / real-demand / score) so `paxLfSource` / `cargoLfSource` still reflect *where* the LF came from; the codeshare trim is observable as a separate breakdown term. `paxLoadFactor *= (1 − paxInterlinePct/100)` and likewise for cargo. Breakdown gains `paxLoadFactorPreInterline`, `paxInterlineSharePercent`, `cargoLoadFactorPreInterline`, `cargoInterlineSharePercent` so the UI can surface "LF would be 70% but partner takes 30%" in the tooltip.

**Aggregator wiring.** `buildRouteRows` accepts new optional `interlineByPair: Map<"HUB-DEST", record>` (or plain `{pair: rec}` from the bulkLoad return shape — the helper detects both). For every row the aggregator computes `{paxPercent, cargoPercent}` via the new `_interlineSharesFromRecord(rec)`: PAX / Y / C / F partner shares sum into `paxPercent` (PAX is the umbrella code; Y / C / F are explicit subclasses); CARGO partners sum into `cargoPercent`; both clamped to 100. The pre-computed shares ride on the row as `row.interlineShares`, which `_applyFleetContext` reads when calling the estimator — so the public re-applier (`applyFleetContext`, called whenever fleet/economics change) doesn't need to re-look up the interline cache.

**Panel cache.** New `this.interlineByPair = new Map()` field, populated in `refresh()` immediately after the route-note bulk load via `RouteAssistantInterlineStore.bulkLoad(dests.map(d => [iata, d]))`. The store returns `{pair: rec}` keyed by "HUB-DEST"; the panel promotes it to a Map for symmetry with `overrideMap` / `routeNoteMap` / etc. Threaded into the aggregator via the new `interlineByPair` input.

**Wave-overlay pill.** `buildSchedule` accepts new ctx field `interlineShareLookup: (destIata) => {paxPercent, cargoPercent} | null`. After `computeConnections` runs, the build walks each `interline`-classified connection and stores `conn.interlineShare = lookup(conn.outboundDest)` when non-null. `_renderConnectionsOverlay` then paints, for every connection with `c.interlineShare`, an SVG `<rect>` (rounded, monospace, semi-transparent fill, stroke matches the bezier style by classification) + `<text>` pill at the bezier midpoint `((x1+x2)/2, (y1+y2)/2)` — the bezier's symmetric control points cancel at t=0.5 so linear midpoint is geometrically exact. Label format: `"Y 30%"` (pax-only), `"C 30%"` (cargo-only), or `"P 30% · C 30%"` (both). Missing records → no pill (cheap short-circuit). Pill data-attributes mirror the underlying connection (`data-aes-interline-pill`, `data-in-seq`, `data-out-seq`) so future hover wiring can target them without re-walking the connection list.

**Cross-hub safety.** `_interlineShareLookup(hubIata)` accepts the hub the wave is being rendered FOR (typically `pickedHub` from the cross-hub picker) rather than always reading `this.hubIata`. The cache is loaded for the primary hub's destinations only, so cross-hub wave views naturally short-circuit to "no pills" — acceptable for v1; future work could lazily augment the cache when the picker switches hubs.

**No new storage keys, no new bus events, no new manifest entries.** All four files were already wired; the slice is pure consumer wiring on top of the H 3b store + the existing wave-overlay + profit-estimator infrastructure. Empty / missing records don't break either consumer (estimator falls through with 0% reduction; renderer skips the pill).

**Verification.** `node --check` clean across all four modified files. Manual smoke path: open the panel on a hub with a recorded interline record (e.g. via 3b.1 right-click → Interlining… or the 3b.1.1 bulk view); switch the wave overlay on; the connection curve to the recorded destination shows the share pill at its midpoint; the row's profit estimate (if visible in fleet/type mode) shows the trimmed LF in its breakdown tooltip. Routes without records render exactly as before — no pill, no LF reduction.

**v1 deferrals.** Cross-hub cache hydration; pill click-through to open the popover (the data-attributes are in place, but no listener wired); explicit class-by-class breakdown in the pill (currently sums across Y/C/F into a single "P" bucket — acceptable because the popover is the source of truth for class-level detail).

### H slice 3b.1.1 — interlining bulk view (settings expander) — shipped this session
**Surfaces in:** Route Assistant settings drawer, between the Carriers expander and the Market Analysis expander. **Files:** `modules/route-assistant/panel.js` only (one call site + two new methods).

Closes the audit-all-routes-at-once gap left by H 3b.1 (per-route popover only): the user couldn't see at a glance how many routes carried interline records, or clear them in bulk if they wanted to start fresh.

**`_renderInterlineRecordsSection`.** Purple-tinted settings expander mounted right after `_renderCarriersSection()` so it lives under the same conceptual "carrier-relations" cluster. Header copy: "Interlining records — Per-route interline / codeshare partners you've recorded across every hub. Layered above the global Carriers contractual cache." Status line populates eagerly via `RouteAssistantInterlineStore.loadAll()` and shows N routes · M partners · last update timestamp. Two CTAs: "View all records ▾" (toggle) and "Clear all" (gated by `window.confirm`).

**Lazy bulk list.** Full grouped list only renders into a scrollable host (max-height 240px) when the user expands the "View all records ▾" toggle. Re-toggle hides without dropping the cached records, so re-opens are instant.

**`_renderInterlineBulkList`.** Renders `loadAll()` records grouped by hub, with the panel's current hub (`this.hubIata`) sorted first and labelled "current panel hub". Each row shows the directional pair (e.g. "JFK-LAX"), partner count, per-class total share summary (Y/C/F/PAX/CARGO with rounded percent), and the record's last-update date. Current-hub rows hover purple and click → `_openInterlinePopover({destIata: dest}, rowEl)` reusing the per-route popover. Cross-hub rows render read-only (no hover, no click handler) with a `title` tooltip pointing the user at "/app/com/scheduling/<HUB>" — necessary because `_openInterlinePopover` is hub-pinned by design (`if (!row || !this.hubIata) return`).

**Clear-all.** Walks every record, splits the directional pair into hub/dest, calls `RouteAssistantInterlineStore.clear(hub, dest)` per pair, surfaces a success toast with the count cleared, and refreshes the status line.

No new storage keys, no new settings fields, no new bus events — pure UI on top of the H slice 3b store and 3b.1 popover that already shipped. Manifest already wires `interline-store.js` + `panel.js` so no manifest change.

**Verification.** Manual smoke (no automated harness):
1. Reload extension; on `/app/com/scheduling/<HUB>` open the panel's settings drawer. New "Interlining records" expander appears between Carriers and Market Analysis.
2. With no interline records: status line reads "0 routes · 0 partners · last update: never"; "View all records ▾" expands to "No interline records yet" empty state; "Clear all" is disabled.
3. Right-click a route in the table → Interlining… → add a partner via the per-route popover. Re-open settings drawer; status line increments and "Clear all" enables.
4. Add records on multiple hubs (switch hubs between adds, the records persist globally). Re-open settings on either hub: bulk list groups by hub, current hub sorts first.
5. Click a current-hub row → per-route popover opens (same one as right-click → Interlining…). Click a cross-hub row → no action; tooltip points to the other hub's scheduling URL.
6. "Clear all" → confirm dialog → status drops to 0 · 0 · never; bulk list (if expanded) drops to the empty state.
7. `node --check modules/route-assistant/panel.js` passes; `python3 -c "import json; json.load(open('manifest.json'))"` clean.

**v1 deferrals.** Cross-hub editing — making `_openInterlinePopover` hub-agnostic so the bulk list could edit any hub's records without navigating away. Acceptable today because hub-switching is a single click in the AS top nav and the bulk list still surfaces every hub's records read-only.

### Branch consolidation — manifest wiring + multi-slice commit chain — shipped this session
**Surfaces in:** git history on `slice/e-integration`. **No code changes** beyond what the prior slices already shipped to disk.

The branch had accumulated ~100 file changes (≈21 K added lines) across roughly a dozen slices whose docs entries here in §1 / §9 already described them as "shipped this session." The on-disk state was internally consistent (`manifest.json` referenced every new module, all 370 referenced files present), but git history hadn't caught up — every entry from World View through Slice 26 Phase 1 through ORS calibration through Tier 3.4 to the customization studio was sitting as untracked / unstaged work on top of the last committed slice (C7 keybindings).

This session reconciled docs against git by splitting the WIP into 9 cohesive slice-bundles and one final wiring commit:

1. `feat(strategy)` — AesStrategy decision engine (panel, journal, learn, outcomes, diff-plan, auto-driver, sister coordination scaffolding).
2. `feat(ra)` — wave + scenario infrastructure (wave-route-fitter, sandbox-scenarios, ORS snapshot store, silent-auto proposers, airport-meta scraper, competitor-income-estimator).
3. `feat(central-hub)` — bus + prefix-store foundation + tile re-render wave across all 8 existing tiles.
4. `feat(fsg)` — Fleet Schedule Grid expansion (host, grid-renderer, dnd, overlays, cockpit, bulk-scraper).
5. `feat(route-launcher)` — Route Launcher pipeline + tile.
6. `feat(competitor-intel)` — Competitor Outline + scrape-orchestrator + counter-aircraft (paired with the `background.js` service-worker importScripts for `background-tab-pool`).
7. `feat(world-view)` — World View tile (network-builder, recommendations, views).
8. `feat(used-aircraft)` — UA scanner enhancements (deal-classifier, market-panel, price-history, scan-lease) + `content_marketScan.js` MarketPanel mount + MAX_PAGES bump 50→200.
9. `chore(misc)` — vfp-reader idempotent guard, score.js denominator fix, schedule-management wave preset integration.
10. `chore(consolidate)` — manifest wiring of all newly-committed modules + customization patch handler in background.js + manifest version bump 0.6.10 → 0.6.11 + this HANDOVER entry + the new `docs/NORTH-STAR.md` and `docs/PLAN-drag-to-schedule.md` reference docs.

**No new invariants.** Every prior invariant in §10 still holds. The two-gate model, form-driver no-submit invariant, pure-function cores, storage budgets, and the bus-as-integration-contract pattern were preserved by every bundle.

**No new storage keys** beyond what the slices being consolidated already documented (those entries in §4 are unchanged).

**Verification.** `git status --porcelain | wc -l` returns 0. `python3 -c "import json; json.load(open('manifest.json'))"` returns 0. `node --check` clean against every staged `.js`. Every file referenced in `manifest.json content_scripts` exists on disk (370 references, 0 missing). AFP / fleets de-dup is preserved (vfp-reader.js carries the new idempotent IIFE guard so the deliberate dual-load doesn't double-mount).

**Why this entry is here.** This is bookkeeping, not new functionality — but a future session reading the git log will see 10 commits land on one date and need a single anchor explaining the shape of the chain. Per NORTH-STAR §11.4, "one slice → one PR by default"; this consolidation honours that retroactively for the in-flight work that had drifted from the rule.

### Auto-Pricing — always-visible status pill + Verify-pipeline CTA — shipped this session
**Surfaces in:** Route Assistant panel statusBar (right edge) + Settings drawer → Auto-Pricing → Tier 3 block. **Files:** `modules/route-assistant/panel.js`. No new modules; reuses `RouteAssistantPricingApplier`, `RouteAssistantPricingApplyLog`, `_silentAutoCollectEligibleRows`, `_silentAutoProposeForRoute`, `RouteAssistantToast`.

Closes the legibility gap from Tier 3.4: every gate, breaker state, and tick result was already persisted, but it all lived inside the collapsible Settings drawer. With Settings closed (default), the user had no signal whether the pipeline was off, dry-run, or live — leading to "I don't know if it's working." This slice surfaces the state on the always-visible statusBar and adds a one-click affordance to confirm the pipeline is alive.

**Status pill (`_renderAutoPricingPill`).** Compact color-coded button on the right edge of the panel statusBar. State priority: breaker > muted > silent-auto live > manual live > dry-run loop > dry-run only > off. Live state shows the active scope set as `M` / `M+B` / `M+A` / `M+B+A` plus `tick Xm` ago when silent-auto is firing. Click jumps the Settings drawer open and scrolls to the pricing diagnostics block (`[data-aes-pricing-diagnostics]`). Refreshed by: `_render()` (every panel re-render), `_silentAutoTick` `finally` (post-tick state), `_attachStorageListener` on `settings` and `routeAssistant:pricingApplyLog*` keys (cross-tab settings flips + new audit-log rows).

**Verify-pipeline CTA (`_runVerifyPipelineCta`).** Button next to the Apply-enabled checkbox in the Tier 3 block. Picks the most-eligible route (smallest |Δ%| eligible candidate via the configured silent-auto proposer; watchlist preferred, falls back to all routes, falls back to no-op against any cached own-pricing row), then calls `applier.apply(hub, dest, prices, {dryRun: true, source: "verify-cta", ...})`. The forced `dryRun: true` opt overrides every gate (apply.enabled, dryRunOnly, liveScopes, breaker except for the breaker's own short-circuit), so the user can verify the pipeline without committing a write regardless of current settings. Result lands in Recent applies as a `dry-run` row; toast shows route + Δ summary or the failure code/message. Exercises GET handshake → form parse → preflight → body construction → log write — every layer except POST.

**Verification (manual).** Open RA panel: pill at far right of statusBar shows current state (default install: ⚪ "Pricing: off"). Open Settings → Auto-Pricing → Tier 3 block: click "Verify pipeline now" — toast within ~2 s reads `Pipeline OK · <route> · Y N→M (±X%) · breaker armed` and a new row appears in Recent applies (dry-run dot). Flip Apply enabled — pill flips to 🟢 "Pricing: live (M)". Flip liveScopes.bulk — pill reads "live (M+B)". Enable silent-auto + liveScopes.silentAuto + click "Run a tick now" — pill reads "live (M+B+A) · tick 0m". AS-side check: open the AS markets page for the applied route; the Y price matches what the apply log says was written.

#### Enabling auto-pricing end-to-end (gate-flip recipe)

The pipeline has three independent scopes (manual / bulk / silent-auto), all locked off by default. Gate sequence:

```
Step 0 — verify pipeline:  RA panel → ⚙ Settings → Auto-Pricing → Tier 3 block →
                           click "Verify pipeline now"
                           ✓ toast → pipeline is alive; advance to Step 1
                           ✗ toast → fix the surfaced error before flipping any gate

Step 1 — manual writes:    flip "Apply enabled" ON
                           keep "Dry-run only" OFF (default)
                           keep liveScopes.manual ON (default)
                           pill should read 🟢 Pricing: live (M)
                           open per-route Apply modal (right-click row → Apply, or
                             from the inspector pane), small Δ on a low-stakes route,
                             watch for `verified` row in Recent applies

Step 2 — bulk writes:      flip liveScopes.bulk ON
                           pill flips to 🟢 Pricing: live (M+B)
                           open the bulk modal, pick 2 routes, small Δ on each,
                             confirm: ≥1 `verified` row per applied route

Step 3 — silent-auto:      flip "Silent-auto enabled" → confirmation modal once
                             (sets silentAutoConfirmedAt; subsequent toggles are silent)
                           flip liveScopes.silentAuto ON
                           click "Run a tick now"
                           pill flips to 🟢 Pricing: live (M+B+A) · tick 0m
                           tick-result line shows applied ≥ 1
```

**Troubleshooting.** Pill 🔴 *breaker* — three consecutive AS 429/503 responses; auto-resets after 10 min, or click pill → Reset breaker. Pill 🔴 *muted* — silent-auto auto-disabled after 5 consecutive failures; click pill → Resume now in the silent-auto block. Pill 🟡 *dry-run loop* — silent-auto is ticking but writes gated; check Apply enabled / Dry-run only / liveScopes.silentAuto. Tick result reads `noEligibleRoutes` — bulk-scrape competitors first (`Sync route data` button), confirm watchlist isn't empty if follow-mode is "watchlist". Tick result reads `staleCompetitorData` — competitor cache > 7 days old; re-run a Markets bulk scrape. Verify-pipeline `noFormContext` — refresh the AS markets page in any tab and retry.

### World View tile — hub-oriented network dashboard — shipped this session
**Surfaces in:** Central Hub (Operations section, priority 30) on `/app/enterprise/dashboard*`. Read-only; no writes back to AS. **New module:** `modules/world-view/` (12 files) + `modules/central-hub/tiles/world-view-tile.js`. **Manifest:** new world-view files added to the dashboard route, plus `route-assistant/wave-overlay.js` (and its `wave-plan-diagnostics.js` / `wave-route-fitter.js` deps), `route-assistant/interline-store.js`, `route-assistant/enterprise-meta-scraper.js`, `route-assistant/contractual-partners-scraper.js`, `competitor-intel/{settings-store,competitor-store,enterprise-scraper}.js` — none of these were previously loaded on the dashboard route.

Closes the gap between the per-route Route Assistant tile and the static Alliance roster: nothing surfaced the network as a whole, oriented to a hub, with competitive pressure + alliance / interline geometry overlaid. The tile composes four panes — **world map** (vanilla SVG equirectangular, bubbles sized by frequency × competition, glyphs by carrier class), **wave Gantt** (synthetic 3-bank schedule fed through `RouteAssistantWaveOverlay.buildSchedule()` + `renderGantt()`), **squarified treemap** (Bruls–Huijsing–van Wijk, sized by `weeklyFlights × competitionScore`), and **alliance + interline recommendations** (transparent formulas; rationale strings cite member counts, hub overlap, contested fraction). Click on any destination emits `focus-route` + `focus-enterprise` on `CentralHubBus` for cross-tile drill-in.

**`modules/world-view/airport-coords.js`.** Hand-curated `IATA → {lat, lon, name}` table (~250 airports across all continents) with an alias map for common AS legacy codes and an equirectangular `project(lat, lon)` helper. Missing IATAs are surfaced as a "{N} dest(s) without coordinates" footer note in the world map; the bubble is hidden rather than guessed.

**`modules/world-view/settings-store.js`.** Per-airline persisted state at `worldView:settings:<airline>` — `{focusedHub, viewMode, expandedSubpanes, lastFocusedAt}`. Mirrors `RouteAssistantSettings` scoping so sister airlines don't stomp each other's focused-hub memory.

**`modules/world-view/network-cache.js`.** TTL-bounded derived-shape cache at `worldView:network:<server>:<airline>:<hub>` (default 1h) with an LRU index `worldView:network:index:<server>:<airline>` capped at 10 hubs per (server, airline) — eviction happens on put. Stores only the derived `WorldViewNetwork` (~3 KB / hub), never the raw snapshot.

**`modules/world-view/network-builder.js`.** Pure function `WorldViewNetworkBuilder.build({snapshot, alliance, partnerCache, ownEnterpriseIds, hub}) → WorldViewNetwork`. Composes destinations from `snapshot.hubs[hub].byRoute[]`, computes per-route `competitionScore = 0.5·normalize(flightCount,0..40) + 0.3·(1−ourPaxShare) + 0.2·normalize(seatCount,0..6000)` and `sizeWeight = log1p(weeklyFlights) · (1+1.2·compScore) · (alreadyScheduled?1:0.6) · (watchlisted?1.15:1)`. Carrier class is derived per-destination from `dominantEnterpriseId` against ownIds + partnerCache + alliance presence. Hard cap of 200 destinations per hub to keep storage and renderers bounded.

**`modules/world-view/carrier-classifier.js`.** Lifts `RouteAssistantPanel._carrierClassifierForFlight()` (panel.js:6678) out of the panel instance into a reusable `WorldViewCarrierClassifier.build({ownEnterpriseIds, partnerByEnterpriseId, leadByDest, fallback}) → (flight) → "own"|"interline"|"alliance"|null`. The wave-pane reconstructs the partner Map from `network.carrierIndex.partnerByEnterpriseId` and passes the closure as `ctx.carrierClassifier` to `buildSchedule()`.

**`modules/world-view/recommend-alliance.js`.** Transparent ranking: `score = reach + 8·feedersAtHub − 3·max(0, overlapHubs−feedersAtHub) − 2·contestedAt`. Buckets cached competitor-enterprise records by `alliance.id || ("name:" + alliance.name)`, takes top-5. Rationale strings cite real member counts and hub overlap so users can sanity-check the ranking.

**`modules/world-view/recommend-interline.js`.** Skips own enterprises, skips current ALLIANCE/INTERLINING partners, hard caps `contestedFraction > 0.35` and `newReach < 4`. Score: `4·feedToHub + 1.5·newReach − 5·overlapDom`. Top-8.

**Views (`modules/world-view/views/*`).** `hub-picker.js` — chip row above the canvas with the hub list from `snapshot.hubs[].iata`, persists pick to `WorldViewSettings`. `summary-strip.js` — auto-fit grid: routes, weekly departures, top-competitor-share, avg pressure (color-toned green/amber/crimson), alliance label, partner count. `world-map.js` — single inline SVG with graticule + region labels; bubbles + glyphs + hub marker (rust ringed); hover tooltip; click emits `focus-route` and (if known) `focus-enterprise`. `wave-pane.js` — synthesizes a 3-bank preset (morning 06–08 / midday 12–14 / evening 19–21) with composition derived from per-destination distance buckets; runs `buildSchedule()` + `renderGantt()`; post-processes `[data-flight-seq][data-direction="outbound"]` bars to mark loose ends with a 1.5px crimson dashed outline; collapses synthetic-window slot warnings; surfaces a connection-cap notice when `build.connections` overflowed. `destinations-treemap.js` — squarified treemap with carrier-class glyph corner + watchlist star + tooltip; click emits `focus-route`. `recommendations-pane.js` — alliance + interline cards with score badges, rationale paragraphs, member chips (alliance) / "in <alliance>" footer (interline); click on a card or member chip emits `focus-enterprise` for cross-tile drill-in.

**`modules/central-hub/tiles/world-view-tile.js`.** `CentralHubWorldViewTile extends CentralHubTile`. `section: "operations"`, `priority: 30`. `watchedStorageKeys()` returns the world-view settings/network prefixes + `aesAlliance:overview` + `aesStrategy:plan:applied`. `loadStatus()` summarizes `N HUBS · M routes · K ally members`. `renderBody()` orchestrates: load `AesStrategy.snapshot()` → load `AllianceOverviewScraper.loadRecord()` → resolve focused hub (instance var > `WorldViewSettings.focusedHub` > `snapshot.hubs[0]`) → build or pull cached `WorldViewNetwork` → render four panes in CSS grid (map full-width, wave full-width, treemap + recommendations bottom 2:1). Subscribes to `focus-route` so cross-tile drill-in (e.g. from route-assistant-tile) auto-focuses this tile's hub. Defensive everywhere: every dependency is `if (typeof window.X !== "undefined")` guarded; failure surfaces a muted seeding hint, never a thrown error.

**Manifest.** Insertion point on dashboard route: between `central-hub/tiles/alliance-tile.js` and `central-hub/host.js`. Order matters — the new world-view files (and their three external deps `wave-overlay.js` / `wave-plan-diagnostics.js` / `wave-route-fitter.js`, plus `interline-store.js` / `enterprise-meta-scraper.js` / `contractual-partners-scraper.js` / `competitor-intel/{settings-store,competitor-store,enterprise-scraper}.js`) load AFTER `tile-registry.js` (line 138) and AFTER `alliance-overview-scraper.js` (line 178). The tile registers itself before `host.js` mounts the shell.

**Verification.** Manual smoke (no automated harness):
1. Reload extension; open `/app/enterprise/dashboard` on a server with at least one hub. Look for "WORLD VIEW" tile under Operations. Header summary like `2 HUBS · 47 routes · 12 ally members`.
2. Expand. Hub picker chips list every entry in `snapshot.hubs[].iata`. Pick another hub — `chrome.storage.local.get(null)` shows `worldView:settings:<airline>.focusedHub` updated, plus a fresh `worldView:network:<server>:<airline>:<HUB>` cache entry.
3. World map: bubbles render at correct lat/lon; pressure colors look right (most fierce = crimson). Hover one — tooltip with destName · weekly flights · pressure %. Click → `competitor-monitoring-tile` auto-expands with a route banner.
4. Wave pane: three banks render. Hover an outbound — matching connection curves brighten (delegated handler in `wave-overlay.js:683`). Loose-end outbounds get a crimson dashed outline; the count appears in the wave-pane footer.
5. Treemap: clicking a tile fires the same `focus-route` emit. Tile color matches map bubble color (same pressure score).
6. Alliance recommendations: with cached competitor enterprises, ≥1 card appears. Rationale cites real reach, feeders, overlap. Click a member chip → `focus-enterprise` lights up alliance / competitor-monitoring tiles.
7. Interline recommendations: cards exclude current `ALLIANCE`/`INTERLINING` partners (verify via `RouteAssistantContractualPartnersScraper` cache for own enterprise records). Card click → `focus-enterprise` cross-drill.
8. Refresh page — `focusedHub` survives (per-airline). Switch hub, refresh again, survives.
9. `chrome.storage.local.remove("worldView:network:<server>:<airline>:<hub>")` then refresh — tile rebuilds in <500 ms.
10. Stress: pick a hub with 80+ destinations — treemap caps cleanly; map shows all bubbles or surfaces a "{N} dest(s) without coordinates" footer.
11. `python3 -c "import json; json.load(open('manifest.json'))"` clean. `node --check` clean on every new file.

**v1 deferrals.** Multi-hub split view (Slice W5) — picker would gain a `compare` toggle, two hubs render side-by-side (small-multiples). Not shipped this slice. Airport coordinate table (~250 entries) covers most AS networks but will surface gaps in unusual servers; expansion is mechanical (drop in more entries). The `WorldViewNetworkCache` evicts cleanly but does not auto-rebuild on TTL expiry — the next tile open triggers the rebuild lazily.

### Auto-Pricing Tier 3.3b — chrome.alarms heartbeat for silent-auto loop — shipped this session
**Surfaces in:** none directly (background driver). Effect: silent-auto cadence is now anchored to a wall-clock alarm in `background.js` rather than the panel's mount-time setInterval, so a backgrounded scheduling tab still ticks reliably and reopening a tab mid-cycle no longer resets the clock. **Files:** `background.js`, `manifest.json`, `modules/route-assistant/panel.js`.

Closes the gap left by Tier 3.3a/3.4: the proposer registry, caps, auto-mute, confirm-on-first-activation modal, and activity feed all shipped, but the cadence source was a `setInterval` in `panel.js` that died with the tab and double-fired on the rare multi-tab case. This slice adds the persistent driver without touching the tick logic itself.

**`background.js` — `aes:silent-auto:tick` alarm.** New section at the bottom registers a single global `chrome.alarms` entry (name `aes:silent-auto:tick`) whose `periodInMinutes` mirrors `settings.routeAssistant.pricing.silentAutoTickMin` (clamped 5–240, matching the panel). `_aesReadSilentAutoConfig` reads the persisted setting in one `chrome.storage.local.get` call; `_aesSyncSilentAutoAlarm` reconciles the existing alarm against intent — creates with `delayInMinutes === periodInMinutes` (so the first fire respects the cadence rather than firing immediately on enable), updates when `tickMin` changes, clears when `silentAutoEnabled` flips false. Reconcile triggers: `chrome.runtime.onInstalled`, `chrome.runtime.onStartup`, and a `chrome.storage.onChanged` listener filtered to changes in the two fields (no-op on every other settings write). On alarm fire, the handler `chrome.tabs.query`s for `https://*.airlinesim.aero/app/com/scheduling/*`, sorts by `lastAccessed` desc, and `chrome.tabs.sendMessage`s a `{type: "aes:silent-auto:tick", firedAt}` envelope to ONLY the most-recently-active tab. Single-tab broadcast prevents the cross-tab dedup race where two panels read a stale `silentAutoLastTickAt` simultaneously and both proceed.

**`panel.js` — alarm-driven tick path.** `_startSilentAutoLoopIfEnabled` now also calls `_attachSilentAutoMessageListener` which registers a `chrome.runtime.onMessage` listener that catches the broadcast and routes to a new `_silentAutoTickIfDue`. The existing setInterval is preserved as an in-tab safety net; it now also calls `_silentAutoTickIfDue` instead of `_silentAutoTick` directly. `_silentAutoTickIfDue` re-reads `silentAutoLastTickAt` via a fresh `chrome.storage.local.get("settings")` call (NOT `this.settings`, which can lag behind another tab's write), and skips when `now - lastTickAt < max(60_000, 0.9 × tickMin × 60_000)`. The `0.9×` factor leaves room for slightly-late alarms or slightly-early setInterval firings without double-ticking. Manual "Run a tick now" still calls `_silentAutoTick` directly so it bypasses the gate by design. `_stopSilentAutoLoop` and `dispose` symmetrically detach the message listener.

**`manifest.json`.** Added `"alarms"` to `permissions`. Version bumped 0.6.10 → 0.6.11.

**Why one tab and not all.** The dedup gate is read-then-decide; with N tabs receiving the broadcast simultaneously, all N would read the same pre-write `lastTickAt`, all N would pass the gate, and N concurrent ticks would race the applier. Sending to only the most-recently-active tab matches user attention (that's the panel they're looking at), and the picked tab's own setInterval still fires at the same cadence as a redundant safety net for the rare case where the message handler is stuck. Multi-scheduling-tab users see exactly one tick per cadence, the same as single-tab users.

**Why setInterval stays.** Removing it would mean a scenario where the alarm fails to register (manifest permission missing on an old install, MV3 quirk, browser policy) leaves silent-auto silent. With setInterval as a fallback, the loop degrades gracefully even when alarms misfire — and the dedup gate ensures the in-tab cadence + the alarm-driven cadence don't compound.

**Verification.** Manual smoke (no automated harness):
1. Reload extension; confirm `chrome.alarms.getAll()` from a service-worker DevTools console returns `[]` when silent-auto is off (default).
2. Open `/app/com/scheduling/<HUB>`, flip silent-auto on (confirm modal), set `Tick (min)` to 5. Check `chrome.alarms.getAll()` — one entry named `aes:silent-auto:tick` with `periodInMinutes: 5`.
3. Wait ~5 min (or set tickMin to 5 then watch DevTools network for ~5min); panel's activity feed shows a tick. `chrome.storage.local.get('settings')` → `routeAssistant.pricing.silentAutoLastTickAt` updates within seconds of the alarm firing.
4. Open a SECOND scheduling tab on the same hub. Wait for the next alarm; only the most-recently-active tab's panel shows the tick in its activity feed (other tab unchanged). Switch focus to the other tab; on the next alarm fire, the OTHER tab ticks.
5. Change `Tick (min)` to 60. Re-check `chrome.alarms.getAll()` — `periodInMinutes` now 60.
6. Flip silent-auto off; `chrome.alarms.getAll()` returns `[]`.
7. Close all scheduling tabs while silent-auto is enabled; reopen one. The alarm fires on its anchored wall-clock cadence (no panel-mount-time reset). The first tick after a fresh mount still respects the 30-second grace timer (so partial scoredRows don't drive an apply).
8. Throttle the scheduling tab to "background" (Chrome DevTools → Performance → CPU throttle 6×) — alarm-driven tick still fires reliably; setInterval would have been throttled to 1/min in this state but the alarm path bypasses it.
9. Manifest validity: `python3 -c "import json; json.load(open('manifest.json'))"`. `node --check background.js` + `node --check modules/route-assistant/panel.js` clean.

**v1 deferrals.** No background-tab orchestration when zero scheduling tabs are open — the alarm fires but the broadcast finds no recipient, the tick is missed. That's "Letter L+" territory (auto-login / hidden-tab orchestrator) and remains explicitly deferred per North-Star §10. The activity feed in non-active tabs doesn't auto-refresh on a sibling tab's tick (the storage listener doesn't watch the silent-auto fields); user opens that tab and the next mount/refresh shows the latest. Acceptable — the user only cares about the tab they're looking at.

### Auto-Pricing Tier 3.4 — proposer registry, undo/batch, live-write scopes, advanced tunables — shipped this session
**Surfaces in:** Route Assistant settings drawer's Tier-3 expander (new Live-write scopes block, new Advanced collapsible subsection, strategy picker now lists three options with descriptions) · the audit-log modal (Undo button per eligible entry, batch click-to-filter pill, undone visual cue) · the dest column of the route table (inline apply-status dot keyed by per-route last-apply state, click to open audit log). **Files:** `modules/route-assistant/silent-auto-proposers.js` (NEW), `modules/route-assistant/panel.js`, `modules/route-assistant/pricing-applier.js`, `modules/route-assistant/pricing-apply-log.js`, `modules/route-assistant/settings-store.js`, `manifest.json`.

Closes four gaps left over from Tier 3.3: (1) silent-auto only had one proposer (`competitor-median`) — `AesStrategy.proposePriceMoves` and `RouteAssistantOrsModel.scanPriceCurve` existed but never reached the applier; (2) an apply was a one-way door — no undo, no batch grouping, no per-route status visibility; (3) every `enabled` flip unlocked all three apply paths at once; (4) several knobs (dedup window, competitor min count, ORS staleness, snapshot age) were hard-coded.

**`RouteAssistantSilentAutoProposers` (new — `silent-auto-proposers.js`).** Pure registry IIFE on `window.RouteAssistantSilentAutoProposers`. Three registered proposers, all conforming to `(route, prices, cfg, ctx) → ProposalResult`. Public API: `list()` returns `[{key, label, description}]` for the strategy picker; `dispatch(strategy, route, prices, cfg, ctx)` selects + invokes; `buildContext({strategy, settings})` is a minimal fallback (panel.js's async `_silentAutoBuildProposerContext` does the heavy work). Result envelope: `{ok, dest, prices, prevY, newY, deltaPct, reason, rationale?, projectedDelta?, objective?}` for hits or `{ok: false, dest, skipReason}` for skips. The three proposers:
  - `competitor-median` — verbatim port of the panel's previous Y-only median tracker. Only behavioural delta: the hard-coded `< 2` competitor floor now reads from `cfg.silentAutoCompetitorMinCount` (default 2).
  - `strategy-objective` — adapter over `AesStrategy.proposePriceMoves`. The tick context pre-builds the snapshot via `AesStrategy.snapshot()` (cached on the panel for `silentAutoStrategySnapshotMaxAgeMin` minutes, default 10) and indexes returned PriceMoves by `HUB-DEST` filtered to `classKey: "Y"`. Per-route lookup is O(1); ratio-converts `toPct/fromPct` to a new absolute Y price; re-applies silent-auto's min-Δ + max-step caps so the user's silent-auto config wins over the strategy module's defaults; carries `rationale` and `objective` onto the apply log entry.
  - `ors-elasticity` — adapter over `RouteAssistantOrsModel.scanPriceCurve`. Hard-skips when the route's ORS cache is older than `silentAutoOrsMaxAgeMin` (default 60); calls `scanPriceCurve(lo:0.7, hi:1.3, step:0.05)` with model params from `settings.orsSandbox.modelParams` and economics from `settings.economics`; picks `optimal.multiplier`; surfaces projected weekly profit delta in `projectedDelta` for the audit log.

Panel `_silentAutoProposeForRoute` now delegates to `RouteAssistantSilentAutoProposers.dispatch` with a defensive fallback to the still-present `_silentAutoProposeCompetitorMedian` for load-order regressions. `_silentAutoBuildProposerContext` is async and called once per tick before the per-route loop. Strategy snapshot build happens lazily — only `strategy-objective` triggers it.

**Apply log observability fields (`pricing-apply-log.js`).** `_cleanRecord` now preserves six new optional fields on every entry: `batchId`, `batchSize`, `undoOf`, `undone`, `undoneAt`, `proposerStrategy`, `rationale`, `objective`. Constructor accepts `dedupWindowMin` (default 5; previously hard-coded). New helpers: `getEntryById(id)` walks global → per-route fallback; `markUndone(id)` flips `undone:true` + `undoneAt` cosmetically (the actual price restore is a fresh apply); `getLastEntry(hub, dest)` returns the most recent per-route ring entry for the badge cache; `getBatch(batchId)` filters the global timeline. The pricing applier's `baseEnvelope` threads `batchId/batchSize/undoOf/proposerStrategy/rationale/objective` from `opts` so they reach the log entry without further plumbing.

**Undo flow (`_undoApplyEntry` in panel.js).** Audit-log row detail surfaces an Undo button when the entry is `verified|posted`, has `prevPrices`, and isn't already undone or itself an undo entry. Click fires a fresh `applier.apply(hub, dest, prevPrices, {source:"undo", undoOf, reason:"Undo <id>"})` — subject to the same preflight, cooldown, and circuit-breaker gates as a manual apply. On success, `applyLog.markUndone(id)` is called and the modal reloads via the `onUndone` callback. The original entry then renders dimmed + struck-through with a "↺ undone" tag; the new undo entry renders with "↺ undo" prefix.

**Batch grouping (`_runBulkPricingApply` + audit modal).** Bulk apply generates one `batchId = "batch-" + Date.now().toString(36) + "-" + rand4` at entry and threads it onto every per-row `applier.apply` call AND the synthetic skipped-by-orchestrator-halt log entry. Audit modal's row detail shows a "🔗 show only this batch" pill when `batchId` is set; click filters the modal to that batch (active-batch banner + Clear filter button). Visually-collapsing batch rows (one summary row → expand to children) was deferred — `getBatch(batchId)` is in place for it but the lighter click-to-filter UI was the right cut for this slice.

**Inline route-row apply badge.** New panel-instance cache `this._applyBadgeMap` (`Map<HUB-DEST, {status, ts, dryRun, undone, source}>`) populated by `_refreshApplyBadgeMap()` from one `log.getRecent()` call. Refreshed on panel mount, after the apply-log preview refresh (which fires after every manual + bulk apply), and after a silent-auto tick that landed any apply. Dest column emits a small `<span data-applybadge-pair="HUB-DEST">` placeholder; `_repaintApplyBadges()` runs at the tail of `_drawTable` (and after each cache refresh) to swap placeholders for coloured dots — green ✓ verified, amber ⚠ posted, cyan 🔍 dry-run, red ✗ failed, grey ↺ undone — with a tooltip showing "Last apply: <state> · <source> · <age> (click to open audit log)". Click stops propagation so the row's existing click handler doesn't double-fire.

**Pre-apply sync timeout (`_raceWithTimeout`).** New helper races a promise against `setTimeout` and resolves to `{timedOut: true, ms, info}` on timeout. Bulk apply's `_orchestratorPreApplySync` call is wrapped against `apply.preApplySyncTimeoutMs` (default 30000); on timeout the apply continues with cached data + a toast warning. The original I/O is not cancelled — that's acceptable for read-only orchestrator work.

**Live-write scopes (`apply.liveScopes`).** New persisted shape `{manual: true, bulk: false, silentAuto: false}`. Enforced at the call sites, not inside the applier:
  - `_runBulkPricingApply` — when `liveScopes.bulk === false`, force `dryRun = true` even if `apply.enabled === true`. Toast "Bulk live writes are off — running as dry-run" surfaces the override.
  - `_silentAutoTick` — `dryRun` now `(apply.dryRunOnly !== false || !apply.enabled || !silentLiveAllowed)`. `silentLiveAllowed = liveScopes.silentAuto === true`. The tick result envelope carries `silentLiveAllowed` for the activity feed.
  - Manual single-route apply + undo — pass through `apply.enabled` as today (manual default unlocked).

This pattern keeps the applier a pure mechanism (it never inspects `source`); the panel-layer policy decides which call sites can write.

**Stale-competitor-data guard.** Silent-auto tick checks `pricing.lastBulkScrapeAt` against `silentAutoStaleCompetitorWarnDays` (default 7) before proposer dispatch. When breached, pushes a `{stage:"warning", reason:"competitor data N days old (>warn)"}` entry into the per-route trace; when `silentAutoBlockOnStaleCompetitors` is true, also aborts the tick with `error.code="staleCompetitorData"` so the activity feed clearly shows why no applies fired. Routes with a missing `lastBulkScrapeAt` get a separate "no competitor bulk-scrape timestamp recorded yet" trace entry.

**Settings UI — Live-write scopes block + Advanced subsection.** Added under the Tier-3 expander in `_renderTier3ApplyBlock`. Live-write scopes is three checkboxes with hover descriptions. Advanced is a collapsed `<details>` with a yellow warning header, a 2-col grid of number inputs (`pricingApplyLogDedupWindowMin`, `preApplySyncTimeoutMs`, `silentAutoCompetitorMinCount`, `silentAutoOrsMaxAgeMin`, `silentAutoStrategySnapshotMaxAgeMin`, `silentAutoStaleCompetitorWarnDays`), and a `silentAutoBlockOnStaleCompetitors` checkbox. The strategy picker dropdown is now driven from `RouteAssistantSilentAutoProposers.list()` so adding a fourth strategy is one entry in the registry plus a deep-merge default. A live hint span under the dropdown shows the selected strategy's description.

**Defaults.** All new settings default to values that exactly preserve the previous baked-in behaviour; existing users get no behavioural drift on upgrade. Deep-merge in `RouteAssistantSettings.load` handles the upgrade transparently — no migration code.

**What did NOT ship this slice (deferred):** (1) Visually-collapsed batch rows in the audit modal (the click-to-filter pill is the lighter substitute). (2) Pre-apply sync timeout for the single-route apply modal — bulk gets the timeout but the per-route refresh button doesn't yet race against the timeout. (3) Wave UI / strategy-tile integration for the new proposers — they're silent-auto and bulk-only this slice.

### Strategy Slice 26 Phase 1 — Journal store + modal panel section — shipped previously
**Surfaces in:** Strategy modal new bottom-of-body "JOURNAL" section (collapsed by default; click to expand into filterable list with click-to-edit reason cells) · `aesStrategy:journal:acct:<id>` ring (cap 750) populated by passive subscriber + active recorders. **Files:** `modules/strategy/journal-store.js` (NEW), `modules/strategy/journal-panel.js` (NEW), `modules/strategy/apply-pipeline.js`, `modules/strategy/learn.js`, `modules/strategy/panel.js`, `manifest.json`.

Closes the "logging mechanism isn't yet finished" gap — the audit + outcomes substrate has been written-to since Slice 4/5, but no surface read it. Slice 26 Phase 1 adds the unified narrative layer the user can scan and annotate. Phase 2 (lesson-miner) and Phase 3 (voice memos) are deferred but the v1 entry schema reserves the slots they'll need (`outcomeRef`, `tags`, `voiceMemoId`) so they don't migrate.

**`AesStrategyJournal` (new — `aesStrategy:journal:acct:<id>` ring, cap 750).** One per-account ring of `{id, ts, accountId, server, airline, action, route, before, after, source, reasonText, voiceMemoId?, outcomeRef?, tags?}` entries. Five action types in v1: `override-save`, `note-save`, `watchlist-toggle`, `apply-decision`, `weight-change`. Public API: `record(opts)`, `addReason(id, text)`, `loadAll(accountId?)`, `clear(accountId?)`, `init()` (idempotent), `bus` (vanilla EventTarget-style — `journal:entry-recorded` + `journal:reason-updated`). Account scoping mirrors `outcomes.js` — scoped writes mirror to the legacy unscoped key only on first miss for the rollout window; new writes land in the scoped slot. Reason text is capped at 200 chars and trimmed on save.

**Hybrid subscription model.** *Passive* via `chrome.storage.onChanged` for the three user-action sources where the storage event already carries enough context (override save → match `routeAssistant:override(:acct:<id>)?:<HUB>-<DEST>`; note save → `routeAssistant:routeNote(:acct:<id>)?:<HUB>-<DEST>`; watchlist toggle → `routeAssistant:watchlist(:acct:<id>)?` blob diff). The subscriber attaches once at module load via `__aesJournalAttached` flag-guarded `init()`. *Active* via direct `record()` calls from two write sites where the storage event would lose semantic context: (a) `apply-pipeline.js` after `_persistAudit/_persistApplied` records one journal entry per applied decision with rationale strings (sliced to 4) + ok/error + domain; (b) `learn.js` after both `setCurrentWeights()` and `resetWeights()` writes records `weight-change` with `{reason, gradient, sampleCount, stepSize, weights}`. Total: 3 passive subscriptions + 2 active call sites. Active is the principled exception per North-Star §4.19 — bridge code stays minimal, but rationale/gradient context lives only in the writer's scope.

**Catch-up scan.** `init()` runs a bounded scan once per page-mount: read the ring's per-action `lastSeenTs`, `chrome.storage.local.get(null)` filtered to override + note keys, back-fill any record with `updatedAt > lastSeenTs`. Covers the case where the user only ever has the scheduling tab open and the strategy bundle isn't loaded — the next dashboard mount picks up the missed writes. Kept simple per the project memory's "throwaway transitional code stays minimal" rule; deletable when Phase 2 ships a more-active recorder.

**`AesStrategyJournalPanel` (new render-only surface).** Pure render fn the strategy modal calls into; composes inline at the bottom of the modal body below the existing learning section. State (expanded / filter / search) lives on the host DOM element so bus-driven re-renders preserve user input. Header always visible: `JOURNAL — N entries [▸|▾]`; click toggles expansion. Expanded body adds: filter dropdown (all / overrides / notes / applies / weight changes / watchlist), search input (route + reason + summary), and a scrollable list capped at the first 50 filtered entries with a "show first 50 of N — refine the filter" footer. Each row: timestamp (tabular numerals, 120 px), action chip (color-coded per action), route, before→after summary, reason cell. **Reason capture is post-hoc only** — no pre-save modal anywhere; click any row's reason cell to open an inline textarea (200-char max), `Save` / `Cancel` / `Esc` / `Cmd-Enter` all wired. Live updates via `AesStrategyJournal.bus`; the listener self-detaches when `host.isConnected === false` so closing the modal can't leak handlers.

**Apply-pipeline integration.** New block right after `_persistApplied()` in `apply-pipeline.js`'s `apply()`: build a `decisionId → decision` map from `allDecisions`, iterate `applied[]`, and `record({action: "apply-decision", route: payload.hub + "-" + payload.dest, after: {decisionId, domain, ok, error, rationale: rationale.slice(0, 4)}})` per decision. Wrapped in try/catch so a journal failure never breaks an apply. Tier-gate short-circuit (preview-only) skips journal recording — those are non-events.

**Learn-module integration.** `setCurrentWeights()` and `resetWeights()` both call a new private `_journal({action: "weight-change", before: {weights}, after: {reason, gradient?, sampleCount?, stepSize?, weights}})` after their existing `_writeHistoryEntry()`. The journal entry duplicates the `learn:weights:history` ring intentionally — journal is the unified narrative; the history ring stays the learn-internal record. Defensive: the journal's `record()` is called with `.catch(...)` and a try/catch wrapper so a journal outage can never block a weight write.

**Strategy modal mount.** Two body-compose paths in `panel.js` (`_refresh()` and the passthrough-plan path inside `open()`) both create a new `_state.journalHost` (border-top divider matching `learningHost`) and call `_renderJournalSection(host)`, which resolves `_scopedAccountId()` and delegates to `AesStrategyJournalPanel.render(host, {accountId})`. Appended to `bodyHost.append(...)` after `learningHost`. Modal close path's `_state` reassignment wipes the host reference — the next open rebuilds.

**Manifest.** Both strategy bundle blocks gain two entries: `journal-store.js` after `learn.js` (so the journal subscriber is attached before `apply-pipeline.js` and `learn.js` start emitting), `journal-panel.js` after `portfolio.js` and before `panel.js` (panel calls into the panel renderer). Dashboard block + `/app/fleets*` block, mirrored. Not added to the `/app/com/scheduling*` block — `chrome.storage.onChanged` fans out to whichever strategy-loaded tab is open; the catch-up scan covers the no-strategy-tab-open case. Validated `python3 -c "import json; json.load(open('manifest.json'))"`; AFP/fleets de-dup empty.

**Eviction-order amendment.** `STRATEGY-ROADMAP.md §IV` line on eviction now reads `learn:outcomes → audit → journal → learn:weights:history → backtest:results`. The journal slots between audit (200 KB) and weights:history (small) so heavy weeks of override-save / note-save / apply-decision activity evict gracefully without ever touching `settings`, `weights:current`, or `plan:applied`. NORTH-STAR §4.10 references `STRATEGY-ROADMAP §IV` rather than carrying the order itself, so no NORTH-STAR amendment needed.

**Verification.** Manual smoke (no automated harness):
1. Reload extension; on `/app/enterprise/dashboard` open DevTools: `await window.AesStrategyJournal.loadAll()` returns `[]` on a fresh install. Open the strategy modal; "JOURNAL — 0 entries ▸" appears collapsed at the bottom.
2. On `/app/com/scheduling/<HUB>` save a per-route override (paxLF .80). Switch to dashboard, refresh modal, expand journal: row `override-save HUB-DEST paxLF →.80`. Click the reason cell, type "test", `Save` — entry persists across modal close/open.
3. With tier `apply-on-confirm` + `priceMovesEnabled`, apply at least one price decision. Journal gains N rows tagged `apply-decision` with rationale on hover (via the truncated rationale[0..4] in the summary).
4. With ≥5 attributed outcomes, click "Run learn cycle" in the Learning section. Verify a `weight-change` row appears with `auto-learn · 5 samples · step 0.05`.
5. Open dashboard + `/app/fleets*` simultaneously. Save an override on a third (scheduling) tab. Both modals' journals pick up the entry within the next refresh (live via the journal bus + cross-tab `chrome.storage.onChanged`).
6. Switch the modal's airline picker to a sister; journal shows only the sister's entries. Switch back; original airline's journal is intact and disjoint.
7. Console: `for (let i=0;i<800;i++) await window.AesStrategyJournal.record({action:"override-save",route:"AAA-BBB",before:null,after:{paxLF:Math.random()}})`. Verify `(await loadAll()).length === 750` (FIFO eviction; cap honored).
8. Close all dashboard/fleet tabs; save 2 overrides on the scheduling tab; close it; reopen the dashboard. Journal contains both entries (catch-up scan picked them up via `updatedAt`).
9. `?aes-debug` flag enables 6 in-file smoke tests in `journal-store.js` covering id shape, reason trimming + cap, override change parsing, note change parsing (legacy unscoped fallback), watchlist add/remove diff, unrelated-key ignore.

**v1 deferrals.** Phase 2 — `lesson-miner.js` joins `journal × outcomes` via the reserved `outcomeRef` slot, clusters by route attributes (incumbent count, distance band, hub, equipment family), and surfaces "Engine learned" cards through `strategy:lesson-mined` events for the executive briefing. Phase 3 — voice memos via `MediaRecorder` + Web Speech API, points the reserved `voiceMemoId` slot at a separate `aesStrategy:journal:voice:<id>` blob (kept out of the entry to avoid bloating the ring).

### ORS — calibration-context capture + snapshot store — shipped this session
**Surfaces in:** Route Assistant panel · ORS settings expander · `routeAssistant:ors:*` (extended) + new `routeAssistant:orsSnapshot:*` keys. **Files:** `modules/route-assistant/ors-snapshot-store.js` (NEW), `modules/route-assistant/ors-scraper.js`, `modules/route-assistant/route-sync-orchestrator.js`, `modules/route-assistant/panel.js`, `modules/route-assistant/settings-store.js`, `manifest.json`.

Builds a (config → ORS rank) calibration dataset alongside the existing live ORS cache. Two layers:

**Context capture on every ORS scrape.** `ors-scraper.js` `scrape()` now accepts `opts.context` (opaque passthrough) and stores it under `record.context`. The route-sync orchestrator gains `opts.contextBuilder` — an async callback called after the schedule scrape with `{hub, dest, scheduleRec}` whose return value threads into the ORS scrape. Panel-side `_buildOrsContext(hub, dest, scheduleRec)` is the single source of truth for "what describes this route at scrape time"; it gathers, all best-effort:
- **`aircraft`** — `typeCode`, `typeId`, `weeklyFlights`, `daysPerWeek` from the freshly-scraped schedule, plus `totalSeats` + `cruiseRangeKm` resolved against `RouteAssistantFleetStore` when the type id matches a slot
- **`priceSnapshot`** — `Y / C / F / Cargo` + `scrapedAt` from the same `_lookupCachedOwnPricing` the apply modal reads
- **`overrides`** — `paxLF / yieldPerKm / cargoYieldPerKgKm / expiresAt / note` from `RouteAssistantRouteOverridesStore.get`
- **`serviceConfig`** — `classMix / serviceLevel / classFares` from `RouteAssistantServiceConfigStore.get`
- **`serviceProfiles.catalogue`** — list of `{id, name}` from `this.serviceProfilesCache` (airline-wide; no per-route assignment exists in our scrape today, so we record the catalogue identity for future "which profile was active at time T" join)
- **`capturedAt`** — ms timestamp

`contextBuilder` is wired into both orchestrator entry points: `_runBulkRouteSync` (the "Sync route + ORS rank" button) and `_orchestratorPreApplySync` (per-route apply modal + bulk apply pre-flight). Failures inside the builder are swallowed — context is metadata; an outage shouldn't sink the actual scrape.

**Snapshot store (`ors-snapshot-store.js`, NEW).** Versioned freeze-frames of the live ORS record so the user can build a reference dataset for time-series compare and model calibration. One record per directional pair holding an array of snapshots:

```
routeAssistant:orsSnapshot:<HUB>-<DEST>                  (legacy)
routeAssistant:orsSnapshot:acct:<id>:<HUB>-<DEST>        (L2+)
  → {
       hub, dest, updatedAt,
       snapshots: [
         {ts, label?, reason, record: {scrapedAt, params, byClass, classesScraped, context}},
         ...
       ]
     }
```

API: `archive(hub, dest, record, {label, reason, maxPerRoute})` (deep-clones via JSON round-trip; enforces `MAX_PER_ROUTE` cap = 30, oldest-by-ts evict), `list(hub, dest)` (lightweight; per-class rank summary + context highlights, drops connection arrays), `load(hub, dest, ts)` / `loadAll(hub, dest)` (full records), `remove(hub, dest, ts)`, `clearForRoute(hub, dest)`, `bulkList(pairs)` for future overview UIs. Account-scoped via `acctKey()`; legacy fallback in `getRecord` matches the route-overrides + sandbox-scenarios pattern.

**Auto-archival.** New panel helper `_archiveOrsSnapshotsFromBulk(bulkResult, reason)` walks the orchestrator's `result.results` and archives each successful per-route ORS record. Wired into both `_runBulkRouteSync` (`reason: "post-scrape"`) and `_orchestratorPreApplySync` (`reason: "pre-apply"` — the highest-signal moment, since pre-apply sync captures the config the user is about to commit). Gated on `settings.ors.snapshotOnScrape` (default `false`) so first-run users don't get flooded; user opts in via the ORS expander.

**Settings.** `ors.snapshotOnScrape: false` + `ors.snapshotMaxPerRoute: 30` join the existing ORS block. `_mergeOrs` already preserves siblings on partial save — no merge logic changes.

**UI.** New row in the ORS settings expander between the param row and the combine row: "Archive ORS snapshots on scrape" checkbox + "Cap per route" number input, both purple-tinted to match the calibration-set theme. Tooltip explains the (config → rank) calibration intent.

**Manifest.** `route-sync-orchestrator.js` (re-)registered + `ors-snapshot-store.js` registered immediately after `ors-scraper.js` in the scheduling content-script block (before panel.js, which references both). The orchestrator's reg was the same hot-fix as last session; this session re-applies it after a linter pass scrubbed it.

**Verification.** Manual smoke (no automated harness):
1. Reload extension; open ORS settings expander; flip "Archive ORS snapshots on scrape" on; reload to confirm persistence.
2. Click "Sync route data + ORS rank" with a few routes visible. After completion, DevTools console: `await chrome.storage.local.get(null).then(o => Object.keys(o).filter(k => k.startsWith('routeAssistant:orsSnapshot:')))` returns one key per scraped route.
3. Inspect a snapshot: `await chrome.storage.local.get('routeAssistant:orsSnapshot:JFK-LAX').then(o => o['routeAssistant:orsSnapshot:JFK-LAX'].snapshots[0].record.context)` shows the populated context block (aircraft / priceSnapshot / overrides / serviceConfig / serviceProfiles.catalogue, where each store had data).
4. Per-route apply modal → click Apply (or Refresh data) on a route. New snapshot lands with `reason: "pre-apply"`.
5. `RouteAssistantOrsSnapshotStore.list("JFK", "LAX")` from DevTools renders the lightweight summary list (most-recent first, one entry per scrape).
6. Trigger 31+ scrapes for the same route (pre-apply syncs work) — confirm cap eviction: oldest snapshot drops once the (N+1)th lands.
7. Untouched: existing live ORS cache (`routeAssistant:ors:*`) — still overwritten on each scrape; snapshots are additive and live in a separate store.
8. Setting OFF (default) — `_archiveOrsSnapshotsFromBulk` early-returns; no `routeAssistant:orsSnapshot:*` keys appear.

Deferred (next slices):
- **Snapshot browser UI** — a per-route drawer listing snapshots with rank-over-time chart + diff vs. current. Foundation in `RouteAssistantOrsSnapshotStore.list/load`.
- **Direct (non-orchestrator) ORS scrape paths** that still bypass context capture — currently every entry point we ship goes through the orchestrator, so this gap is theoretical until someone wires a one-off scraper UI.

### Strategy Slice 6 — Auto route creation actuator — shipped this session
**Surfaces in:** Strategy modal new-route decisions flip from advisory → applicable (the user's tick + per-domain `routeCreationEnabled` flag now wires through to a real apply path) · existing route-creation proposer (`route-creation.js` from Slice 3) is unchanged. **Files:** `modules/strategy/route-creation-applier.js` (NEW), `modules/strategy/diff-plan.js`, `modules/strategy/apply-pipeline.js`, `manifest.json`.

Closes the loop on the user's stated requirement: *"automate the creation of the new routes."* Slice 4 surfaced route-creation proposals as advisory ("ships in Slice 6"); this slice ships the actuator that turns each `RouteCreation` into real legs on a real aircraft via the existing `AesAfpFleetApplyOrchestrator` pipeline. Strategy never POSTs directly — `route-creation-applier.js` is a pure orchestration layer that picks an aircraft, builds legs, and hands off.

**`AesStrategyRouteCreationApplier` (new).** API: `apply(creation, snapshot, opts)` returns `{ok, aircraft, legs, result, error?, reason?}`. Aircraft selection priority: tails of one of `creation.proposedTypeIds` whose `currentLocationIata` matches the creation's hub, sorted by remaining wear-budget headroom (`maxWeeklyBlockHours - weeklyHoursLast7d`) descending — fallback to any tail of the right type when no in-hub candidate exists. Leg construction: N round-trip pairs (N = `proposedFrequency` clamped [1, 14]) at evenly-spread departure times across the operating window 06:00–22:00, return time = depart + flight time + 60-min ground turn, computed via `AesStrategyScoring.flightTimeMin`. Each leg is tagged with `_strategy.newRoute = true` for downstream audit visibility. `pickAircraft()` and `buildLegs()` are exposed as pure helpers for `?aes-debug` smoke tests + future test harness.

**Failure modes** (returned via `{ok: false, reason}`): `bad-input` (missing hub/dest), `missing-orchestrator` (AFP pipeline not loaded), `no-fleet` (no eligible tail), `leg-build-failed` (zero distance / missing speed), `missing-server`, `orchestrator-aborted` (AFP tier gate dormant or fleet-apply explicitly aborted), `orchestrator-threw` (exception in the orchestrator). The strategy apply pipeline maps these into per-decision audit entries so the user sees exactly why an attempted creation didn't land.

**Apply-pipeline integration.** New `_applyRouteCreations(decisions, ctx, snapshot, applied, skipped, opts)` sub-pipeline mirrors the price/service/crew shape: actuator-missing → skipped, missing server/snapshot → audited as failure, otherwise iterates and per-decision dispatches to the applier. Independent of subsequent (one creation failure does NOT abort the rest). **Sequence is now schedules → service → price → crew → routeCreation** — route creation runs last so a partial earlier failure doesn't risk creating routes on a half-applied plan. Snapshot is required for aircraft picking; pipeline composes one lazily when `opts.snapshot` is missing, AND reuses that snapshot for the Slice 5 outcome-recording step (saves a third snapshot fetch on a busy apply).

**`diff-plan.js` flip.** Route-creation decisions are now `applicable: true` whenever `proposedTypeIds.length > 0` (which the proposer always guarantees — the gate stays defensive). The advisory fallback note now reads "No fleet type covers the route's distance — add an aircraft of suitable range" instead of the Slice 4 placeholder. Existing decisions that rendered as advisory before the upgrade re-render as applicable on first mount post-upgrade.

**Tier + per-domain gate (unchanged from Slice 4).** Real apply requires (a) tier ≠ `preview-only` AND (b) `settings.strategy.routeCreationEnabled === true`. The applier itself does NOT enforce either — `AesStrategySettings.canApply(s, "routeCreation")` is the single point that combines both, called from `apply-pipeline.js` before the `_applyRouteCreations` dispatch. The downstream `AesAfpFleetApplyOrchestrator` still enforces the AFP tier gate (`autoScheduler.enabled === true && tier === "apply-on-confirm"`), so even with strategy-side gates flipped, AFP can still abort — flagged in the report as `result.aborted`.

**Verification.** Manual smoke: reload extension; on `/app/enterprise/dashboard` open the Strategy modal; route-creation decisions now show ticked checkboxes (no advisory badge); flip tier `apply-on-confirm` + `routeCreationEnabled` checkbox; tick at least one new-route decision; click Apply. The applier picks an aircraft (visible via `chrome.storage.local.get(["aesStrategy:plan:applied"])` → `applyReport.applied[i].result.aircraft.aircraftId`), builds N round-trip leg pairs, dispatches via the AFP orchestrator. With AFP tier gate dormant the report shows `result.reason: "orchestrator-aborted"`. With AFP tier gate flipped on AND a real aircraft of the right type at the hub, the orchestrator routes legs through the existing `apply-batch` pipeline and AS receives the new flight numbers. `?aes-debug` flag enables 10 in-file smoke tests covering `_spreadDepHours` / `_formatHHMM` / `pickAircraft` (in-hub, fallback, no-fleet) / `buildLegs` (shape, freq, pricePct threading, _strategy marker, zero-distance bail).

**v1 deferrals (carried into Slice 12 / Slice 6.1):** network-effect scoring (route-creation bonus when the new route connects to an existing partner's destination via codeshare/IL — Slice 12). Slot availability check (consult `aesCanopy:slots:<airport>` from L11 once that ships). Real-time vs game-time aware operating window (the 06:00–22:00 spread is local-clock; very late-night arrivals bunch on the day boundary; Slice 6.1 will add "wave-aware" spread that consults `schedule-management/presets-store.js` for the user's preferred wave layout). Auto-application (`tier === "apply-auto"` driving creation without a user click) — defer to Slice 16's executive briefing where the auto-apply driver lives.

### Strategy Slice 5 — Closed-loop learning (outcomes + finite-difference learner) — shipped this session
**Surfaces in:** Strategy modal new "Closed-loop learning" section (counters, step-size slider, pause toggle, run-cycle / capture-pending / reset-weights buttons, current-weights table, recent history list) · dashboard "Strategy" tile new "Learning" card (outcome counts + last weight change). **Files:** `modules/strategy/outcomes.js` (NEW), `modules/strategy/learn.js` (NEW), `modules/strategy/apply-pipeline.js`, `modules/strategy/panel.js`, `modules/central-hub/tiles/strategy-tile.js`, `manifest.json`.

**`AesStrategyOutcomes` (new — `aesStrategy:learn:outcomes` ring, cap 100).** Records `{outcomeId, planId, applyTs, server, airlineCode, before, after, afterTs, weights}` per applied plan. The `before` measurement is captured by `apply-pipeline.js` immediately after a successful apply (skipped when `learningEnabled === false` so the ring doesn't fill with no-attribution-coming records). The `after` is captured by `tryCaptureAfter({minWindowHours, snapshot, plan?})` which walks the ring and attributes every outcome older than the window. `measure(snapshot, plan?)` is exposed as a pure function — extracts only the fields the gradient estimator needs (`{ts, cashBalance, weeklyResult, fleetCount, legCount, orsAvgY, perRouteCount, paxLfMean, predictedWeeklyProfit?, predictedOrsAvg?}`) so storage cost stays bounded regardless of snapshot size. `countReady(minWindowHours?)` returns `{recorded, ready, attributed}` — the modal + tile read this for the counter chips.

**`AesStrategyLearn` (new — `aesStrategy:learn:weights:current` + `aesStrategy:learn:weights:history` cap 52).** `getCurrentWeights()` returns the merged weight set — stored override deep-merged into `AesStrategy.DEFAULT_WEIGHTS` so missing keys always have a default. `setCurrentWeights(weights, reason, extra)` persists + appends to history with `{ts, reason, before, after, gradient?, stepSize?, sampleCount?}`. `resetWeights(reason)` removes the override + history-archives. `learn(opts?)` is the closed-loop entry: gates on `settings.learningEnabled` (force-override via `opts.force === true` for the modal's "Run learn cycle" button), reads `AesStrategyOutcomes.loadAll()`, filters to attributed outcomes, requires ≥ `minSamples` (default 5), then runs the finite-difference gradient estimator described in the roadmap — for each weight key, partition outcomes into above-median vs below-median by that weight's value, compute mean profit-delta per cohort, gradient = (aboveMean − belowMean) / range, then step `newWeight = clamp(currentWeight + stepSize × gradient, 0, 2)` per key. Step size defaults to `settings.learningStepSize` (0.05); the modal slider writes through to settings.
