No save-back yet. Pareto-frontier recommendation panel ("Shift A's Wave 2 by +30 min → +12 connections from B, costs N intra-account connections"). | ~900 |
| | **L11** | Slot model + storage | `aesCanopy:slots:<airport>` per (kin, time-bucket); slot-pressure column on dashboard; capacity warnings in editor; slot record write-through invariant — schedule shifts re-derive slot records in the same `chrome.storage.local.set` batch. | ~400 |
| | **L12** | Wave-edit save-back + interline-aware solver | Tier-3 two-gate write path into per-account `ScheduleStore`; per-account confirm modal + cross-account audit log + 60s undo window; `schedule-builder` gains optional canopy weight `λ × Σ cross-account connection bonuses` alongside intra-account profit objective. | ~900 |
| **5 Polish** | **L13** | Migration cleanup + Letter L+ readiness | Ambiguous-data resolution modal, per-account storage breakdown in options ("Storage by account" + per-class breakdown), "delete account + data" confirmation flow, legacy-key prune helper (off by default; surfaces as "Reclaim ~Nkb"); hooks pre-staged for Letter L+ canopy auto-login (deferred). | ~400 |

Total ~8500 LOC across 13 slices. Multi-month, multi-session. **L1–L8 deliver standalone value** (registry, affiliations, DNA, combined supply, dashboard, fleet balance — a clear milestone after which the user can run the federation analytically). **L9–L12 unlock the operational coordination breakthrough.** L13 ships last.

**Migration is one-shot, idempotent, additive.** On first load, `migrateLegacy()` enumerates every `(server, airline)` tuple seen in existing `<server><airline>aircraftFleet` keys and registers them. Class B/C/D legacy keys are copied (not moved) into their `:acct:<id>:` namespaced form, owner inferred from `isOurs` markers when available, falling back to most-recently-active account. Multi-account users with pre-existing data see a **"Confirm legacy data ownership" modal** before passive migration locks in. `aesAccounts.migrationVersion = 1` marks complete. Legacy keys NEVER deleted by Slice L1–L5 — they remain as fallback for any reader not yet refactored. L6 prunes after one release cycle confirms safety.

**Letter L+ (canopy auto-login, deferred):** the original Letter L brainstorm — encrypted credential vault, hidden-tab orchestrator that auto-logs-in expired sessions, scrapes, closes — is the natural follow-on. Slice L1–L6 leave the registry shape ready for it: `aesAccounts.accounts[id]` simply gains a `credentials` field + a `sessionState`, and a future `modules/canopy-orchestrator/` reads the same per-account namespaced keys. ToS / CAPTCHA / MFA risks remain for that slice; re-evaluate when the user is ready. Don't ship without explicit user confirmation that AS rules permit it.

**New §10 invariants this work introduces (cumulative across slices):**

*Foundational (L1–L3):*
- Registry single-writer rule — only `background.js`'s `account:touch` handler writes `aesAccounts`. Content scripts send messages, never `chrome.storage.local.set` directly on this key.
- accountId resolution order — `currentAccountIdSync()` reads `window.__aesAccountId` set during `helpers.js` bootstrap; off-AS-page callers read `aesAccounts.viewingAccountId`. Stores must NEVER `await` registry lookup before their first read in a page lifecycle (race condition).
- Full-replace stores' accountId round-trip — `RouteOverridesStore`, `ratingAlphaStore`, etc. retain full-replace semantics; every caller capturing `prev` for Undo must capture `accountId` too so restore lands in the right account even if the chip switched mid-session.
- Migration idempotency — `migrationVersion` gate; rerunning is a no-op. Legacy keys are read-only fallback during slice L1–L7 — never overwritten by namespaced data.
- Class A keys stay global by design — adding `:acct:<id>:` to demand / distance / typeSpec / fuelPriceIndex would be a regression; cross-account sharing is the whole point.
- Per-aircraft AFP keys (`<server>aircraftFlights<aircraftId>`, `aircraftFlightPlan:state:<server>:<aircraftId>`) NOT re-scoped — aircraftId is unique per server. Only the AFP audit-log timeline gains `accountId`.

*Affiliation + DNA (L4–L5):*
- **Affiliation graph is the single source of "kin / partner / competitor".** The old `isOurs` flag stays for ORS scrape time but classification for combined-supply, cannibalization, gateways, and effective-competition all reads `aesCanopy:affiliations` via `AesCanopyAffiliations.classify(enterpriseId)`. Forking the rule (e.g. one site classifies via `airlinePrefix`, another via affiliation graph) creates silent disagreements that surface as inconsistent dashboard numbers.
- **Affiliation user-override precedence is absolute.** `userOverride: true` flag is non-negotiable; auto-classification only fills empty slots. "Reset to detected" clears the flag. Stale-override surfacing is visual-only — never silent overwrite.
- **All kin are equal — no parent/child hierarchy.** No `parentEnterpriseId` field. Kin display ordering = lastSeenAt; if hierarchy display is desired later it's a UI label, not a data-model change.
- **DNA hybrid scope: `effectiveDna(accountId) = deepMerge(template, override)` at leaf level.** Every consumer (DNA-fit scorer, drift detector, default-settings template) reads via the resolver. Direct reads of just template or just override are bugs because they miss the federation/diverge intent. Drift always against effective, never template.

*Cross-account analytics (L6–L8):*
- **Combined-supply functions are pure.** No DOM reads, no chrome.storage reads. Inputs are `(routes, accounts, affiliations)` triples. Tests can hit the function without mocking storage.
- **EnterpriseId enrichment on competitor rows is best-effort.** When markets-page scrape doesn't return enterpriseId per row, fall back to carrier-prefix match against kin's known prefixes. Don't silently fail; surface "competitor enterpriseId missing — using prefix match" in the row tooltip.
- **DNA-fit scoring is normalized 0–1.** Every dimension contributes 0–1; weighted-sum produces overall score. Adding new DNA dimensions extends the contributor list without changing the score's range.

*Cross-account scheduling (L9–L12):*
- **Wave-editor save-back uses Tier-3 two-gate pattern.** `apply.enabled === true` AND `apply.dryRunOnly === false`, mirroring `pricing-applier`. Per-account confirm-before-save + cross-account audit log + 60s undo. Don't add a third path that bypasses both gates.
- **Cross-account schedule write requires kin-tagged accounts.** A wave save touching A and B requires both as `self`. Allied / interline are observation-only — never write their schedules. Hard error otherwise.
- **Slot record write-through.** Whenever a kin's schedule shifts, `aesCanopy:slots:<airport>` records for affected hours must be re-derived in the same `chrome.storage.local.set` batch as the schedule write. Decoupling = silent contract break.
- **Gateway audit results are advisory.** Audit emits projected connection counts from MCT + timing math, not from observed booking. After any wave save, banner prompts re-scrape ORS to confirm. Tooltip explicitly says "projection — re-scrape ORS to confirm."

### Tech debt
- ~~**Rename `ticket-price-scraper.js`** → `schedule-page-scraper.js`~~ ✅ Done. Class is now `RouteAssistantSchedulePageScraper`; storage prefix `routeAssistant:ticketPrice:` left as-is per the §10 stable-key invariant.
- ~~**Family-card grid override-walk**~~ ✅ Done. `TypeFamilyMap.familyList(overrides)` now folds `block.typeFamilyOverrides` into the matching family card; truly unknown labels still land in Custom.
- **Existing presets with multi-daily routes** — Wk column reads legacy cache via `frequencyPattern` fallback; one Sync click re-writes to the new `dailyFlights[]` shape.
- ~~**`routeAssistant:topRoutes` freshness**~~ ✅ Done. `displayUsedAircraftScanner()` now installs a singleton `chrome.storage.onChanged` listener (250ms debounced — RA writes both global + per-hub keys per render) that re-pulls `loadDealContext(server)` and re-renders results. Previous listener is removed on each remount via `window._aesTopRoutesListener`.
- ~~**Break-even daily-hours assumption**~~ ✅ Done. `MarketScanDealMetrics._blockHoursFor(row)` now picks from `BLOCK_HOURS_BY_CATEGORY` (commuter/turboprop/regional 8h, narrowbody 12h, widebody 14h) keyed off `row.familyCategory`. Falls back to the 10h `DAILY_BLOCK_HOURS` constant when category is missing.
- **Carriers SSR fragility** — `RouteAssistantCarriersScraper` parses the initial HTML returned by `fetch(flightsfrom.com/<HUB>-<DEST>)`. When flightsfrom doesn't SSR the carrier list (or changes their markup), the record stores `parserNotes` describing what was tried and `carriers: []`. The panel falls back to the plain `airlineCount` integer in that case. A child-tab worker (parallel to `content_flightsFrom.js`) would let the SPA hydrate before extraction; defer until we observe SSR failing in practice.
- **Competitive-intensity bands** — three-band thresholds (1 / 2-3 / 4+) are intuition-tuned, not data-fit. If users want different cutoffs the boundaries should move into `settings.routeAssistant.carriers` as `{lowMax, midMax}`.
- **Enterprise-meta selector fragility** — `enterprise-meta-scraper.js` walks 4 strategies (`[class*='banner']`, `[class*='avatar' / 'logo' / 'profile']`, `og:image`, header-img) and disambiguates banner-vs-avatar by candidate width + class keywords. AS markup tweaks could break this. Records carry `parserNotes` so the failure mode surfaces in the popover footer ("Some banners/avatars missing — open Settings → Carriers → Sync enterprise data"). When the heuristic genuinely lands the wrong image in the banner slot, the user can right-click the popover row in a future slice to re-tune.
- **Tabbed view — per-tab weights not stored** — switching the tab gates which scoring fields contribute, but the *weight* for each field is shared across all three tabs. A user who wants `weeklyFlights × 1` on Pax but `× 0.5` on Cargo today can't express it. If demanded, lift `settings.routeAssistant.scoring` from a single block to `{all: {…}, pax: {…}, cargo: {…}}` with deep-fill on load.
- **Demand depth — elasticity small-N regressions** — `RouteAssistantDemandDerivator._elasticity` requires ≥ 4 valid (capacity, price) points after dropping zeros. New routes / dormant lanes will hit this floor and return null with a `derivationNotes` line. Acceptable now (the column shows em-dash); a future tweak could fall back to a global cross-route elasticity prior.
- **Demand depth — PAX double-count risk** — `_pickPaxSeries` prefers explicit `byPayload.PAX` over summing Y+C+F when all three are captured. AS's PAX historic is itself the sum of those three, so we pick one or the other deterministically. If AS ever exposes PAX as a *different* aggregate (e.g., excluding F), the demand pool would be wrong by ~5–10%. Verify against a live route before trusting.
- **Demand depth — inventory parser fragility** — `parseInventoryHtml` walks every table on the page and relies on header keywords (Economy/Business/First/Cargo, Total/Capacity/Sold/Booked). AS markup tweaks could break this. Records carry `parserNotes` describing what was tried. Real-world tuning likely needed against an actual `/app/com/inventory/<HUB><DEST>` HTML sample.

---

## 10 · Open invariants — don't break without checking

- Every `chrome.storage.local` key prefix in §4 is **stable**. Other features and saved user data read them.
- `RouteAssistantPanel.SCORING_FIELDS[i].field` ↔ `RouteAssistantPanel.COLUMNS[i].field` for any variable that's both scored and displayed.
- `RouteAssistantSettings.load()` always returns a fully-populated object (deep-merged with `_defaults()`). Never assume a freshly-loaded settings object lacks a field.
- `_pairKey` in `distance-resolver.js` is **alphabetically sorted** — distance is symmetric. `_pairKey` in `schedule-page-scraper.js` AND `yield-history-store.js` is **directional** — price/freq/profit differ by direction. Different rule per file; easy to mix up.
- `enterprise-meta-scraper.js` is **NOT keyed by route** — its cache key is `routeAssistant:enterpriseMeta:<enterpriseId>`. The same record is reused across every route the enterprise competes on. Don't add HUB/DEST to the key; you'd just blow up storage with duplicates.
- `contractual-partners-scraper.js` is keyed by `routeAssistant:contractualPartners:<enterpriseId>` where the enterpriseId is **your own**, not a competitor's. The record's *contents* tell you who that enterprise's partners are; the popover then matches partner ids against visible competitor ids. Fetching every visible competitor's `?tab=1` would (a) waste fetches and (b) miss cases where the partnership is asymmetric — only your own page reflects who YOU have agreed with. Don't repurpose the scraper for competitor-pulls without changing the consumer logic.
- F slice 3 popover glyphs read `this._partnersByEnterpriseId` which is built by `_applyCachedContractualPartners()`. That method is called from `init()` after `_applyCachedEnterpriseMeta()` and again after every refresh. Adding new ways for partners data to land must call it (or the glyphs go stale until the next mount).
- **Tabbed view (`viewMode`)** — `RouteAssistantPanel._columnModes(col)` and the SCORING_FIELDS `modes` array gate visibility per tab. The "all" tab MUST keep every field active (preserves the pre-tabbed score blend for users who don't switch tabs). New scoring/column entries that should hide on focused tabs need an explicit `modes` array; entries without one default to all three modes.
- **Demand depth — `historic.byPayload`** — DON'T fold the `byPayload` map back into a top-level `{periods, capacities, prices}` shape. The pre-K shape stored only ECONOMY; the new shape stores all five payloads under one record. `_migrateHistoricRecord` reads either shape on load, but new writes MUST use `byPayload`. Adding a top-level `payload` field again would silently break the per-class derivation in `demand-derivator.js`.
- **Demand depth — `useRealDemandForLF` defaults false** — flipping it on shifts every $/flt and $/wk number on routes that have demand-depth data. The `_renderDemandDepthSection` toggle prompts a confirm on first activation; that prompt MUST stay so users see the warning before profit numbers drift.
- **Demand depth cache key non-collision** — `routeAssistant:inventory:<HUB>-<DEST>` is **directional**. Cargo aircraft on the route still see the same key — there's no per-aircraft variant. Don't sub-key by typeId; the inventory page is route-level, not aircraft-level.
- Yield Feedback now supports four attribution modes (`frequency` / `distance` / `equal` / `per-flight`) and a separate `deltaMode` axis. **Cumulative vs delta** toggles per-tail averaging mode (subtracts the prior snapshot's cumulative profit/flights from each tail's current numbers). **Per-flight (exact)** is a different code path entirely — it ignores tail averages, joins each aircraftFlights envelope with `<server>flightInfo<flightId>.money.CM5.Total`, and aggregates by `{originIata, destinationIata}` from the envelope. Routes with no measured flights fall back to per-tail frequency-weighted, never silently fail. The baseline blob (`routeAssistant:yieldBaselines`) is updated on every snapshot regardless of the active mode so flipping into Delta mode doesn't need a warm-up pass.
- `dailyFlights` is always a 7-element array (Mon→Sun); `weeklyFlights = dailyFlights.reduce(+, 0)`; `daysPerWeek = dailyFlights.filter(>0).length`.
- The aggregator's `applyFleetContext(rows, null)` clears every fleet-derived field. Calling with `null` must reset to a clean Phase-1-style row.
- Cargo revenue defaults to OFF (`cargoYieldPerKgKm: 0`). Don't change the default; existing users would see surprise number shifts.
- Family-card grid walks `block.typeFamilyOverrides` via `TypeFamilyMap.familyList(overrides)` so override-redirected labels appear in their target family card. Scan-time controller still honors overrides too.
- Phase 1 + 1.5 + 2 + 2.5 + 2.6 + 2.7 are all in production with user-tuned settings. Don't rip features without confirming.
- `routeAssistant:topRoutes` is **slim by design** (only the fields the Used Aircraft Scanner consumes). Adding fields here grows the per-render storage write — confirm a new consumer before extending.
- `routeAssistant:lastSnapshot:<HUB>` row keys are the **row property names** (`orsRatingGapToTop`, not the spec name `ratingGapToTop`). The diff lookup uses these literally — renaming the row field without renaming the snapshot key would silently kill the badge for that column on the next mount. The list of tracked fields lives in one place: `RA_DIFF_TRACKED` at the bottom of `panel.js`. Add a column → add an entry there → add `_appendDiffBadge(td, "<field>", row)` to the render closure. Don't fork the list.
- The diff baseline is **loaded once per (mount, hub) pair**, stored in `this._diffPrevSnapshot`. Within a single mount/hub session the baseline does NOT refresh from storage — even though `_drawTable` overwrites the snapshot every render, in-memory deltas remain anchored to "first read on mount". Hub change in the dropdown invalidates and re-loads via `this._diffBaselineHub` mismatch. Don't add a `chrome.storage.onChanged` listener for the snapshot key; the design intent is mount-anchored deltas, not live ones.
- `MarketScanDealMetrics.decorate()` mutates the row it's given. `MarketScanResultsTable._enrichDeal` always passes a fresh `Object.assign({}, r)` copy — new callers must do the same to avoid stomping `this.rows`.
- `RouteAssistantCarriersScraper._pairKey` is **directional** — `<HUB>-<DEST>` matches the flightsfrom URL pattern. Different from the symmetric `_pairKey` in `distance-resolver.js`. Easy to mix up.
- `RouteAssistantPanel._showCarrierIntensity` is a static class field updated in `_syncRenderContext()` from `settings.carriers.showCarrierIntensity`. The Cmp column's `render` closure reads it at draw time — adding new closures that depend on the carriers settings should follow the same pattern (don't reach back into a panel instance).
- **Markets-page split storage.** `routeAssistant:markets:*:<HUB>-<DEST>` is **4 sibling key families**, not one blob. Always read via `RouteAssistantMarketsPageScraper.bulkLoadCache(pairs, {families: [...]})` so the read amplifies into one combined `chrome.storage.local.get`. Writers MUST go through `saveAllRecords` — direct writes risk leaving the families out of sync.
- **ORS `byClass` is the source of truth for all rank flavors and the composite.** `routeAssistant:ors:<HUB>-<DEST>.byClass` holds per-class records (`ECONOMY` / `BUSINESS` / `FIRST`), each carrying its own rank flavors + ratings + connection list. The composite/headline metrics (the values shown in the panel's ORS / RkNS / Gap / Y / C / F columns) are **computed at read time** in `_applyCachedOrs` from `byClass` using the user's `combineMethod` + class weights. **NEVER store the composite** — settings changes (preset, weights, combine method) re-project without re-scraping. Adding a new rank metric should be a pure-function read against `byClass`, never a re-scrape.
- **ORS lazy migration** — legacy single-class records (pre-this-session) keep working via `RouteAssistantOrsScraper._migrateOrsRecord` which wraps the flat `{rankAny, …, connections}` into `{byClass: {<original payload>: {…}}}` at read time. Pure read-time, never writes back. The next `scrape()` overwrites with the new shape.
- **Per-class column visibility (`orsClassY` / `orsClassC` / `orsClassF`)** is gated by BOTH `settings.ors.showPerClassColumns` AND `!settings.compactView`. Compact view always hides them regardless of the toggle. The gate logic lives in `_activeColumns` next to the existing group-level gates.
- **Composite formula skips null per-class values.** If FIRST has 0 connections (regional route, no F cabin), its metrics are null and the composite excludes it from the weighted average — the formula doesn't divide by 0 or treat null as zero. Same for any class not yet scraped.
- **ORS Wicket per-route handshake.** Each `RouteAssistantOrsScraper.scrape()` does GET → POST. Do NOT try to share a wicket session across the bulk batch — page-version IDs increment per interaction; reusing a stale session returns a `PageExpiredException` HTML page that parses as zero results (silent corruption). The 2-request handshake cost is acceptable at concurrency=2 / stagger=1500ms.
- **Tier 3 two-gate model is non-negotiable.** A real `RouteAssistantPricingApplier.apply()` POST requires BOTH `settings.routeAssistant.pricing.apply.enabled === true` AND `apply.dryRunOnly === false`. The applier instance enforces both — even if a caller passes `{dryRun: false}`, the instance's `dryRunOnly` overrides. Slice 3.1 shipped with both blocked; slice 3.2 flipped `dryRunOnly`'s default to `false` but `enabled` stays opt-in. Don't add a third path that bypasses these (e.g., a "force-apply" debug helper). The two gates have separate semantic meanings — kill switch (user's "off" intent) vs slice gate (the codebase's "ready" intent) — collapsing them would break the safety contract.
- **Tier 3.2 circuit breaker persists through settings.** The applier instance carries `_consecutiveErrors` for the session; `circuitBreakerTrippedAt` lives in `settings.routeAssistant.pricing.apply` so a tripped breaker survives panel remount + page reload. `_persistPricingBreakerTrip` writes; `_persistPricingBreakerReset` clears on the first verified/posted apply. Don't move trippedAt to in-memory only — a 429 storm should make Apply unreachable until cooldown expires across every page load. Don't move `_consecutiveErrors` to settings either — it resets on the first non-rate-limit terminal path (verified, posted, or even non-rate-limit failure) and shouldn't bleed across instances. The Step-0 cooldown gate at the head of `apply()` is dry-run-exempt: dry-run is a pure GET that's safe even while AS is rate-limiting POSTs.
- **Tier 3 per-apply Wicket handshake.** `RouteAssistantPricingApplier.apply()` MUST do its own fresh GET on every call. Don't try to cache the form context across applies — same reason as the ORS scraper, page-version IDs invalidate per interaction. Caching the cached-ownPricing snapshot for the modal's input defaults is fine; caching the form action/hidden fields for the POST is NOT.
- **Tier 3 `prevPrices` comes from the live GET, not the cache.** The applier's audit-log `prevPrices` field is the `currentPrices` parsed from the same GET that harvested the form context. Don't substitute `cachedOwn.prices` — a stale cache would make Undo restore to the wrong baseline. The cache is only used to seed modal inputs (UX nicety).
- **Tier 3 fingerprint dedup is at the apply-log layer, not the applier.** `RouteAssistantPricingApplier.fingerprint(hub, dest, prices, scope)` is deterministic; the applier always computes one and passes it to the apply log. The log dedups on identical fingerprint within a 5-min window (default `dedupWindowMs`) by merging into the head entry's `count` rather than adding a new entry. Don't move dedup into the applier — the apply pipeline always wants to run (the user might be retrying after fixing a preflight blocker; we want the new attempt visible), and the audit trail is where dedup belongs.
- **Tier 3 cooldown reads only successful entries.** `RouteAssistantPricingApplyLog.getLastSuccessAt(hub, dest)` walks the per-route ring looking for the most recent `verified` or `posted` entry. `dry-run` and `failed` entries are SKIPPED — a dry-run shouldn't gate a real apply, and a failed write shouldn't gate a retry. Cooldown enforcement is in preflight (`cooldownMinPerRoute`); changing the success-statuses set requires updating both.
- **Tier 3 submit button name MUST be in the body.** Wicket disambiguates which submit handler fires by which submit-button name appears in the body. Default `submit-prices` (apply prices only); `p::submit` (apply prices + settings); `submit-settings` (apply settings only — irrelevant to Tier 3). Skipping the submit name would cause AS to reject the form silently. Slice 3.1's `buildBody` always includes one.
- **Tier 3 General Settings round-trip.** `parseFormContext` captures the current selected `value` for each of the 5 General Settings selects. `buildBody` sends them back unchanged unless the caller overrides via `opts.settings`. Why: the markets-page form posts EVERYTHING in the panel; sending only price fields would clear terminals, service profile, boarding/cargo preference. Don't drop the round-trip; an apply that wipes those is a HIGH-severity regression.
- **Tier 3 missing-class round-trip.** When `prices` is partial (e.g., only `{Y: 150}`), `buildBody` sends every other class at its current value. Don't change this to skip the missing fields — Wicket forms are atomic; the response would clear the un-sent prices to zero or default.
- **Tier 3 apply log shape is contract.** Adding new fields is fine (the cleaner drops unknowns gracefully); renaming fields will silently break the panel's "Recent applies" preview list (it reads `e.status` / `e.prevPrices` / `e.newPrices` / `e.source` / `e.ts`). The preflight sub-record's shape (`{blockers, warnings, deltas, percentDeltas}`) is also contract — the modal renders blockers/warnings independently.
- **Tier 3.3b silent-auto cadence has two cooperating drivers.** `chrome.alarms` (registered in `background.js`, name `aes:silent-auto:tick`, period = `silentAutoTickMin`) is the primary cross-tab/persistent driver. The panel's `setInterval` is a deliberate in-tab safety net; both call `_silentAutoTickIfDue` rather than `_silentAutoTick` directly. Removing setInterval would leave a single-point-of-failure if alarm registration is suppressed (manifest perm missing, MV3 quirk). Removing the alarm would lose persistence + cross-tab anchoring. Don't merge them.
- **Silent-auto alarm broadcasts to ONE tab, not all.** `chrome.alarms.onAlarm` in background.js sorts scheduling tabs by `lastAccessed` desc and `chrome.tabs.sendMessage`s the highest-ranked one. Broadcasting to every tab races on the read-then-decide dedup gate (multiple panels read the same pre-write `silentAutoLastTickAt`, all pass, all proceed). The single-tab pick + each tab's setInterval safety net is the correct design — don't switch back to a fan-out broadcast.
- **`_silentAutoTickIfDue` re-reads from `chrome.storage.local`, not `this.settings`.** Cross-tab dedup requires the freshest possible `silentAutoLastTickAt`; `this.settings` is only updated when the panel saves OR when `chrome.storage.onChanged` propagates, both of which lag the actual write. The fresh-read is one extra storage round-trip per tick (cheap at 5–240 min cadence). Don't substitute `this.settings.pricing.silentAutoLastTickAt` to "save the round-trip" — you'd reintroduce the race.
- **Manual "Run a tick now" intentionally bypasses the dedup gate.** `_silentAutoTickNow` calls `_silentAutoTick()` directly, NOT `_silentAutoTickIfDue`. The user's explicit click should always run a tick even when another tab ticked moments ago. Don't route the manual CTA through the gate; you'd silently no-op the user's click.
- **Alarm sync is reconcile-on-change, not write-on-every-settings-save.** The `chrome.storage.onChanged` listener in background.js filters to changes in exactly two fields (`silentAutoEnabled`, `silentAutoTickMin`). Without that filter, every settings save (panel-width drag, filter chip toggle, etc.) would re-sync the alarm. Don't widen the filter; the two-field comparison is the contract.
- **ORS circuit breaker is mandatory.** First HTTP 429/503 increments `_consecutiveErrors`; 3 in a row halts the bulk run, persists `settings.ors.circuitBreakerTrippedAt`, and disables the bulk button for `circuitBreakerCooldownMs` (default 10 min). Never silently retry through rate limits.
- **`expiresAt` MUST round-trip through every override save path.** `RouteOverridesStore.save` is full-replace (line ~80 of route-overrides-store.js) — fields not in the saved record are intentionally cleared. The full override editor passes `expiresAt` from its input. The profit-modifier popover, Q2 bulk modal, and all Undo restore closures explicitly carry `prev.expiresAt` through. Skipping any of these wipes the user's TTL on incidental yield/LF tweaks. New override-write call sites should follow the same pattern: `expiresAt: prev.expiresAt != null ? prev.expiresAt : null`.
- **`row._expired` / `_expiresSoon` decoration runs once per render in `_renderRows`.** The walker reads `row.override.expiresAt` against `Date.now()`. Expensive: O(rows). The Dest cell render closure consumes the flags but doesn't re-derive them — keeps the closure cheap. Don't move the decoration inside `_buildTable` or you'll re-run it on every cell render.
- **Aggregator's `isExpired` gate is the SINGLE point that suppresses applying.** The estimator gets `null` when expired; the row keeps the record so the UI can render. Don't add a second gate (e.g. in the panel) — they'd diverge. The aggregator's gate is what the profit numbers depend on; the panel decoration is purely visual.
- **`_detectStatusTransitions` runs on EVERY render, not just first mount.** A route's status can flip between renders (filter chip toggle changes scoring → status changes). The dedup inside `RouteAssistantStatusHistoryStore.appendTransition` AND the in-memory check in `_detectStatusTransitions` ensure no duplicate writes when status hasn't actually moved — but transitions DO get logged when the user's filter changes the computed status mid-mount. That's correct: the user is observing a real change in their workflow's view of the route. Don't move the call to mount-only; you'd miss filter-driven transitions.
- **`row._statusHistory` is decorated even when no transition is appended.** The St cell render closure depends on it being set for every row; the decoration runs unconditionally inside `_detectStatusTransitions`. Don't gate the decoration on "did we append?" — the closure will silently fall through to "no history" for ALL rows on subsequent renders.
- **First observation appends `{from: null, to: <status>, at: now}`.** Tooltip renders this as "first observed" rather than "was X before". Don't seed an empty transition or backfill from `routeAssistant:lastSnapshot:<HUB>` — the `lastSnapshot` is one-mount-old and may not reflect the actual prior status. Single-source-of-truth: the history accumulates from real observed transitions.
- **`MAX_TRANSITIONS = 20` is a hard cap.** Older entries are trimmed via `splice` on every append once the array exceeds 20. Don't bump the cap without re-checking storage growth at scale (50 hubs × 200 routes × 40 bytes per entry × N).
- **`_selectedRoutes` is ephemeral — NOT persisted to chrome.storage.** Selection lives in memory across sort/filter/refresh changes within a single panel mount. The drop-on-filter-intersection at the end of `_renderRows` is the single point that prunes hidden routes; don't add a per-call-site filter-aware drop or you'll fire the toast multiple times per render. Future "saved selection sets" feature should add a SEPARATE persisted store; do not bolt it onto `_selectedRoutes`.
- **`_selectAnchorDest` is tracked by destIata, NOT by index.** Sort changes reorder the visible rows; a destIata-keyed anchor stays valid through sort. If the anchor's row gets filtered out (no longer visible), the intersection-at-end-of-`_renderRows` clears the anchor too — shift+click thereafter falls back to plain toggle.
- **`RouteOverridesStore.save` is FULL-REPLACE, not merge.** Q2 bulk apply MUST read prev records first, build a merged record per-route preserving untouched fields, and pass the full record to `save()`. Skipping this loses untouched fields per route — silent data-loss bug. The `_applyBulkOverride` helper bakes this contract in; future bulk-write call sites should follow the same shape (capture prev → merge → save → wrap in `_undoableSave`).
- **Q2 parallel saves use `Promise.all`, NOT serial.** `chrome.storage.local.set` is contention-free between routes; serial saves at N=50 take ~250ms, parallel ~25ms. The Undo restore path is the same. Don't refactor to serial unless a future store gains contention-sensitive behaviour.
- **Q2 calls `_recomputeProfit()` ONCE at the end of the batch.** Per-route profit recompute would multiply render cost by N. Same for the Undo restore path. Don't move the call inside the per-route loop.
- **U11 hover card is a singleton** — only one card exists in DOM at a time. `_showRowPreview` calls `_hideRowPreview` first to clean up the previous one. Don't track multiple cards; the dwell + grace timing means a second card would only ever flash on cursor cross-overs and we'd still want one to win.
- **U11 dwell + grace timers must be cancellable.** `mouseenter` cancels `_rowPreviewLeaveTimer`; `mouseleave` cancels `_rowPreviewTimer`. Cursor enters card → cancels `_rowPreviewLeaveTimer`. Forgetting any of these creates flicker (card opens then closes mid-cursor-move). Test by hovering a row, moving cursor along the row's height, then to the card.
- **Q13 reads BOTH `routeAssistant:topRoutes` AND `routeAssistant:topRoutes:<HUB>`** — but only the per-hub key for cross-hub matrix data. The legacy single-key consumer (Used Aircraft Scanner deal-metrics) is unaffected; both keys are written on every render so the legacy contract survives. Don't drop the legacy key without auditing every consumer.
- **Q13 X-axis ordering uses the CURRENT hub's score** as primary sort. Destinations that exist in other hubs but not the current one fall to the alphabetic tail. This makes the current hub's row "look right" first; switching hubs (via Q15 chip click or browser navigation) re-renders with the new hub's score order at the next mount.
- **Q13 cell click opens a NEW TAB** (`window.open(..., "_blank")`) — does NOT mutate the current tab's panel state. Selection survives. The new tab is a separate AS scheduling page that the panel will mount fresh on.
- **Render branch order in `_renderRows` is wave → sandbox → heatmap → table.** All four can be independently toggled; the highest in the chain wins when multiple flags are on. Don't reorder; existing test verifications depend on Wave winning, then Sandbox, then heatmap.
- **`AesStrategyJournal` is narrative, NEVER a source of truth.** The journal records every override / note / watchlist toggle / applied decision / weight change for the user's review and the future lesson-miner (Slice 26 Phase 2). It is NOT consulted by `scoreRoutes` / `allocateFleet` / `apply-pipeline` / `learn`. Don't add a code path that gates a decision on what the journal says — that would let a corrupted ring silently distort scoring or block applies. The audit ring (`aesStrategy:audit`) and outcomes ring (`aesStrategy:learn:outcomes`) remain the canonical apply / attribution sources.
- **Journal active-record sites are exactly two.** `apply-pipeline.js` (one entry per applied decision, after `_persistApplied`) and `learn.js` (one entry per `setCurrentWeights` / `resetWeights` write). Adding a third active site requires confirmation that the storage event for that write would actually lose semantic context; the default for any new journal-able event is the passive `chrome.storage.onChanged` subscriber in `journal-store.js`. Active sites also MUST be wrapped in try/catch so a journal failure cannot break the underlying write.
- **Journal entry schema is contract.** The five action types (`override-save`, `note-save`, `watchlist-toggle`, `apply-decision`, `weight-change`) and the reserved Phase 2/3 slots (`outcomeRef`, `tags`, `voiceMemoId`) are stable. Phase 2's lesson-miner reads `outcomeRef` to join entries to outcomes; renaming or repurposing those slots would force a backfill migration. Adding a new action type is fine (the panel renderer falls back to the raw action string) — but document it in the SCOPE_PREFIX and journal-store.js's `ACTIONS` set in the same PR.
- **Journal's `chrome.storage.onChanged` subscriber attaches once per page load** via the `__aesJournalAttached` flag inside `journal-store.js`. Manifest order MUST keep `journal-store.js` ahead of `apply-pipeline.js` and `learn.js` (so its passive subscriber catches their first write) and ahead of `panel.js` (so the modal section can reach `window.AesStrategyJournal`). The catch-up scan inside `init()` runs once per mount and is bounded by `lastSeenTs`; don't move it inside the storage event listener — the scan is per-mount, not per-event.
- **Reason capture is post-hoc only.** No write site (override save, note save, applied decision, weight change) is allowed to block on a reason prompt. The user adds reason text by clicking the row's reason cell after the fact. A pre-save modal would touch every override / note write site for a 200-char field most users skip — too much bridge code for the value. New journal-able events should follow this pattern.
- **`RouteAssistantAlertEvaluator.evaluate` is pure.** No DOM, no I/O, no `chrome.storage` reads. The panel passes `rules` (already loaded) and `rows` (already decorated with `_diff` + `_starred`) and gets back a list of triggered alerts. Rule cooldown is checked AGAINST the rule's own `lastFiredByRoute` map — the evaluator doesn't write back; the panel calls `RouteAssistantAlertRulesStore.recordFired(...)` after firing each toast. Don't put DOM/storage code inside `evaluate` — it's the contract that lets the panel call it on every render without performance fear.
- **`_alertFiredThisMount` is per-mount, NOT per-render.** Reset in `refresh()` and never elsewhere. The panel re-renders many times per mount (sort change, filter tweak, slider drag). The set keeps the same rule+route from re-firing during those intra-mount re-renders. The store-level cooldown (`rule.cooldownHours`) handles cross-mount dedup. Don't reset the in-memory set inside `_renderRows` or you'll spam toasts.
- **Alert rule `operator` semantics are strict.** `increased_by` / `decreased_by` read `row._diff.<field>` — null when there's no diff baseline (first mount on a hub). `above` / `below` read the raw row property. Don't mix — adding a new operator requires extending the evaluator's `_evalOnRow`, the available-fields catalog, AND the settings UI dropdown.
- **Alert rule field catalog is in `RouteAssistantAlertEvaluator.availableFields()`.** Adding a new alertable field means: (a) ensure the field is populated on `row.*` by the aggregator/applyCached path, (b) if it should be diff-trackable, add it to `RA_DIFF_TRACKED` in panel.js + the `_loadDiffSnapshot` shape, (c) append to `availableFields()`. Skipping (b) silently disables `increased_by` / `decreased_by` for that field with no error — the rule fires on raw `row[field]` only.
- **`RouteAssistantToast.progress` toasts are sticky by default (duration: 0).** Don't forget to call `complete(...)` or `dismiss(...)` when the operation finishes — orphaned progress toasts will sit forever until the user clicks ×. The bulk-scrape pattern always wraps in try/catch with a `complete({type: "error"})` in the catch + a final `complete({type: "success" | "warn"})` in the finally path. Future progress-toast call sites should follow the same shape.
- **`RouteAssistantToast._history` is in-memory only.** The `action.fn` reference is kept by reference in the history array; functions aren't serialisable so persisting to chrome.storage would lose them. The notification-center popover relies on the in-memory reference to re-fire actions. Closing the tab clears the history — that's documented in the popover footer. If you ever DO persist (e.g. for cross-tab visibility), drop the `action` field on serialise; deserialised history entries should render as click-disabled rows.
- **`_history` cap is enforced via `shift()` on every `show()`.** When `MAX_HISTORY = 50`, the oldest entry slides out as the newest comes in. Don't replace shift with splice or filter — shift is O(1) on most JS engines for sub-100 element arrays, and the array's reference identity must stay stable for the popover's iteration.
- **N2 popover is not a toast itself** — it's a separate floating panel anchored to the 🔔 button. It doesn't go through `RouteAssistantToast.show` (would create infinite recursion in the history). Outside-click + Escape teardown is local. Don't refactor it to use the toast primitive.
- **Q1 chips read `settings.filters[<field>]` directly.** `_applyFilters` consults `f.watchlistOnly` / `f.lossMakers` / `f.hasOverride` / `f.hasNote`; `_renderRows` consults `f.onlyChanged` post-decoration. Adding a new chip = (a) new field in `settings.filters` defaults, (b) one branch in `_applyFilters` (or `_renderRows` if it depends on `_diff`), (c) one row in the chips array in `_renderQuickFilterChips`. Don't fork the field name between defaults + filter + chip.
- **Q15 `recentHubs` cap is 5.** Hard cap on the array, NOT just on the chip-row render. The settings-store load-merge enforces the cap on every read so a stale value > 5 in storage gets trimmed. Don't bump the cap without re-checking the chip row's overflow behaviour (5 fits with the existing controls-bar layout; more would wrap).
- **Q15 Alt+N keyboard shortcut skips when focus is in input/textarea/contentEditable.** Test this — hub-swap eating Y-yield typing during override editing would be a major regression. The handler explicitly checks `t.tagName === "INPUT" || t.tagName === "TEXTAREA" || t.isContentEditable`. Don't drop the guard.
- **`_attachHubShortcuts` is idempotent.** Multiple constructor calls don't pile up listeners (the `if (this._hubShortcutHandler) return` guard ensures one handler is registered at a time). `_detachHubShortcuts` clears it. `dispose()` must call detach to prevent leaked listeners across panel re-mounts.
- **Progress toast pattern across all 6 bulk-sync sites.** Same shape: open `progressHandle` on entry, pump `progressHandle.update(...)` from inside `onProgress`, call `progressHandle.complete(...)` with terminal type at the end. Multi-phase scrapes (markets, demand) compose phases as a rolling bar over `pairs.length × phaseCount` total work — phase index times pairs.length offsets the local done into global done. Don't fork that pattern; future bulk-sync sites should mirror it line-by-line.
- **`RouteAssistantToast.update` is keyed by id, not by handle.** Calling `update(id, ...)` on a dismissed toast is a no-op. The handle returned from `progress()` is just a thin wrapper; if you store it across an async boundary, check `RouteAssistantToast._registry.has(id)` before spamming updates.
- **`_undoableSave` capture-then-perform contract.** The helper does NOT capture prev state itself — that's the caller's job. Read the prev value INTO A CLOSURE, then call `_undoableSave({perform: ..., restore: ...closure references prev...})`. This keeps the helper store-agnostic; each store has its own shape and the caller owns the read. If you skip the capture, the Undo will overwrite to whatever current state is at click time (which may have moved on if the user did several saves in a row). Future call sites should follow the same `const prev = store.get(...) || null` pattern.
- **`_undoableSave` toasts use `duration: 6000` by default.** Longer than the default 3.5s because Undo decisions take time. Don't shorten without confirming the user feels they had a chance to react.
- **`RouteAssistantToast` and `_noteToast` serve different jobs.** `_noteToast(msg, isError)` writes to a single inline status line under the panel header — used by long-running operations that want a persistent breadcrumb of "what's happening right now" (scan in progress, demand seeding, syncs). `RouteAssistantToast.show(msg, opts)` is for *transient floating* feedback — confirmations, undo affordances, errors, results. Both are kept; future features pick the right one for the situation. Don't replace `_noteToast` call sites with `RouteAssistantToast` blindly — the in-line status is load-bearing for some UX flows.
- **`RouteAssistantToast` calls are guarded by `typeof RouteAssistantToast !== "undefined"` at call sites in panel.js.** This is defensive: if the toast module ever fails to load (manifest order regression, a future split-build), the panel still functions without throwing. New call sites should follow the same guard pattern. Do NOT make the panel hard-depend on the toast global.
- **U15 `data-group` attribute is contract.** Every column header `<th>`, group header `<th>`, and body `<td>` MUST carry `data-group="<group>"` matching the COLUMN_GROUPS map. The hover highlight is just one consumer; future column-band features (filter chips, column-group toggle) will reuse the same selector. Don't drop the attribute when adding new column-render closures.
- **`_applyFilters` honours `settings.searchQuery` AFTER status / distance / fleet-flyable filters but BEFORE scoring.** The order matters because score normalisation re-bases against the visible row set; filtering before scoring keeps the score column meaningful (otherwise hidden routes would still influence the normalisation). Adding new filters should land in `_applyFilters` (between distance and search), not in `_drawTable`.
- **ORS Sandbox is read-only against AS and chrome.storage.local except `settings.routeAssistant.orsSandbox`.** The sandbox NEVER scrapes new ORS / markets data on its own (the user runs Sync ORS / Sync market analysis from the existing settings expanders); NEVER writes prices back to AS (Tier 3 is a separate feature); NEVER mutates `routeAssistant:override:` / `routeAssistant:routeNote:` / `routeAssistant:serviceConfig:` (the slice 2 "Save scenario as override" CTA is the future write path). The only persistence the sandbox does is its own settings block: `enabled`, `lastRouteIata`, `lastScenario`, `modelParams`, `perRouteTemperature`. Adding write-back here without a dedicated review is the highest-risk regression vector for slice 1.
- **`RouteAssistantOrsModel.project` is pure.** Inputs are immutable references (don't mutate `route.orsByClass[...].connections`); the model copies before mutating ratings. Adding side effects (storage writes, fetches, DOM access) to the model breaks the contract — the panel calls `project()` at 60Hz during slider drag (rAF-coalesced) and any I/O would tank perf. Keep it pure.
- **The model's rating-shift formula is `linear-in-percent`, NOT log-ratio.** `newRating = base − α_price × (newPrice − observedPrice) / observedPrice + α_comfort × Δcomfort`, clamped to `[0.5×base, 1.5×base]`. Outside ~30% deviation the formula is only directionally accurate; the panel surfaces this via the notes footer when clamps fire. Don't switch to log-ratio without retuning α_price defaults — the user's calibration intuition is built around the linear default.
- **Softmax share uses the `numeric-stable form`: `exp((rating − maxRating) / T)`.** Rating values regularly hit 100–200; without the max-subtraction, `exp(rating / T)` overflows at T < 10 and underflows when summed against zero ratings. Don't refactor away the max-subtraction.
- **`RouteAssistantOrsModel.MAX_FOR_SOFTMAX = 50`** — hard cap on connections fed to the softmax. Past rank #50 the contribution is <0.1% share at default T, and AS's pagination cutoff matches. Bumping this past 50 makes the rank list slower without changing share materially; lowering it below ~20 starts dropping legitimate competitors.
- **ORS Sandbox panel-mode mutual exclusion with Wave View** is enforced by branch order in `_renderRows` (Wave View checks first, then ORS Sandbox, then default table). Both flags can be `true` in settings simultaneously — Wave wins. Don't add a third mode that breaks the table-fallback path.
- **Right-click row menu split** preserves the existing override editor as the FIRST menu item (default action), with "Open in ORS Sandbox" second. Reordering would break user muscle memory built up over Phase 2.6 (the override editor has been right-click-only since the per-route LF/yield override shipped). The 2-item menu via `_openRowContextMenu` is the new gating point — don't bypass it back to a direct call.
- **Per-route T storage is opt-in, not auto-populated.** `settings.routeAssistant.orsSandbox.perRouteTemperature[<HUB>-<DEST>]` only gains an entry when the user clicks the Calibrate-T button on that route. The button refuses calibration when `marketShare` data is missing, the user's enterprise isn't in the leaderboard, or the freshness window between marketShare and ORS data is > 7 days — surfacing those failures by amber banner text rather than silently writing an inaccurate T. Adding an "auto-calibrate every visible route on mount" affordance in slice 2 must keep the same refusals.
- **`calibrateTemperature` returns the `closer endpoint` when the target share isn't bracketed in `[1, 200]`.** Observed shares that the rating-driven softmax can't reproduce (e.g. our 24% with rating-rank-#2 in a 5-connection list) hit this branch and return T=200 (the smooth boundary). The panel banner shows the value verbatim — the user reads "T=200" as "share isn't fully explained by rating differences alone." Slice 2 could surface this as an explicit warning, but slice 1's behaviour is correct and honest.
- **Wave overlay module is read-only against ScheduleStore; the panel's `_saveWaveScheduleToStore` is the SOLE write path.** `RouteAssistantWaveOverlay.buildSchedule()` runs `ScheduleBuilder.assignRoutes` + `evaluateFlights` + `computeConnections` in-memory; nothing persists. The `💾 Save schedule` CTA in the wave header strip (slice 2) is the single explicit-action gateway into `ScheduleStore.save` — it validates (preset present, airline loaded, no validation errors, ≥1 flight), constructs a fresh `ScheduleStore.newSchedule(...)` record from the cached build, mirrors `ScheduleBuilder.build()`'s warning surfacing for unplaced + shortfall, and wraps in `_undoableSave` so a misclick is one toast click from `ScheduleStore.remove`. **Do not** add silent-save anywhere else (e.g. an auto-save inside `_renderWaveOverlay`, a save embedded in `buildSchedule`, or a "save on preset change" hook); collapsing the explicit-action gate breaks the slice-1 read-only contract that other panel modes (sandbox, heatmap, table) still depend on.
- **Wave overlay reads from `this.scoredRows` AFTER the existing scoring + sort + watchlist decoration.** The early branch in `_renderRows` is intentionally placed at the bottom of the method (just before `_drawTable`) so Wave View honours filters, view mode, AND the watchlist's float-to-top behaviour without forking that logic. Don't move the branch earlier — you'd lose the watchlist promotion of pinned routes into the top-N candidate set.
- **`ScheduleBuilder.optimizeAssignment` treats composition counts as CAPS, not floors.** When `assignRoutes` dispatches to the optimiser via `opts.optimize`, the hill-climb may leave a wave below its `wave.composition[bucket]` if moving routes elsewhere lifts the connection score. Per-wave shortfall is reported only when the bucket's input queue exhausted (genuine "couldn't fill"); waves intentionally drained to feed a higher-scoring wave do NOT show shortfall. Forced (📌) overrides are pinned — the optimiser tracks them separately via the `movables[]` array and never touches them. The hill-climb's score uses arrival/departure window MIDPOINTS as a fast approximation of the actual transfer gap; the final flight-by-flight count from `computeConnections` may differ by 5–15% on presets with wide windows. Don't switch the optimiser to spread-aware exact scoring without profiling — the 200-iter climb runs ~M²×B times per iter and exact scoring would push that into perceptible UI hitches. The 200-iter cap is intentional: presets typically converge in 5–15 iters; the cap is a safety net for pathological inputs (e.g. many waves with overlapping bridges).
- **`RouteAssistantInterlineStore` is layered ABOVE `RouteAssistantContractualPartnersScraper`, not folded into it.** Contractual is the global "who you have agreements with" cache (per-enterprise, non-directional, keyed by your-own enterprise id); interline-store is the per-route operational layer (`<HUB>-<DEST>` directional, array-of-partners record). They serve different questions — "is this carrier a partner of yours at all?" vs "on this route, what's the per-class share?" — and merging them would (a) explode the contractual record from O(partners) to O(partners × routes), (b) lose the asymmetric directionality (reverse-direction codeshares aren't always symmetric), (c) couple the layers so a fresh contractual scrape would clobber operational data. The 3b.1 / 3b.2 consumers (UI popover, wave-overlay annotation, profit estimator) read BOTH stores: contractual gates "is this even a valid partner option" and interline-store carries the share. Empty interline records are dropped from storage (no tombstones); consumers that walk `bulkLoad` results must `if (map[pair])` cheaply rather than expecting a key for every visible route.
- **Interline-store dedup key is `(partnerEnterpriseId, productClass)`, NOT `partnerEnterpriseId` alone.** A single carrier may have separate codeshare arrangements per cabin class (e.g. you cede 30% of Y to AA but block-buy 10 C seats from them). The store's `addPartner` upsert preserves both via the composite key; `removePartner(hub, dest, pid)` without `productClass` removes ALL of a partner's classes at once — convenient for "the agreement ended, drop everything" — but `removePartner(hub, dest, pid, "Y")` removes only the Y-class entry. Don't change the dedup key without auditing every call site; a single-key dedup would silently overwrite a partner's other classes when adding a new one.
- **`_pairKey` rule (recap, growing list).** Symmetric (alphabetically sorted): `distance-resolver.js`. Directional (`<HUB>-<DEST>` literal): `schedule-page-scraper.js`, `yield-history-store.js`, `route-overrides-store.js`, `carriers-scraper.js`, **`markets-page-scraper.js`** (this session, all 4 families), **`ors-scraper.js`** (this session), **`interline-store.js`** (this session). Different rule per file; easy to mix up.
- **`isOurs` detection** in `RouteAssistantOrsScraper.computeRanks` does **flight-number set match first, carrier-prefix fallback second**. Per user direction (option C of three offered). The flight-number set comes from the `<server><airline>schedule` cache (per-route `flightNumber: {FN1: {…}, FN2: {…}}` keys). For multi-enterprise users, both schedules contribute prefixes via `getOurCarrierPrefixes`.
- **Watchlist storage is global, not per-hub.** `routeAssistant:watchlist` holds every starred pair across every hub the user has touched. The panel filters by `this.hubIata` at render time (only pairs prefixed `<currentHub>-` decorate as starred). Don't add a per-hub key — the multi-hub design is intentional so a future "Watched routes" view can surface pinned routes across hubs in one place. Pair key is **directional** `<HUB>-<DEST>` uppercased; matches the orientation of `route-overrides`, `ticket-price`, `yield-history`.
- **Route notes are per-(hub, dest) and directional.** `routeAssistant:routeNote:<HUB>-<DEST>` matches the directional orientation of `route-overrides` etc. Empty/whitespace `text` field collapses to a `remove()` so deleted notes don't leave tombstones in storage. The Dest column 📝 icon is **always rendered** (subdued when empty) — this is a deliberate deviation from the spec's "when present" wording so users can discover the affordance for empty routes. Don't gate the glyph on `row.routeNoteText`; gate only its opacity + tooltip text.
- **Route note save is in-place, not a refresh.** `_openRouteNotePopover`'s Save handler mutates `row.routeNote` + `row.routeNoteText` directly + updates `this.routeNoteMap` + calls `this._renderRows()`. Don't call `await this.refresh()` on save — that would reload fleet, distances, schedule, demand etc. just for a text edit. The storage listener (`_attachStorageListener`) is also NOT wired to the `routeAssistant:routeNote:` prefix for the same reason; adding it would cause every save to refire `refresh()`.
- **Watchlist trigger fields piggyback on `RA_DIFF_TRACKED`.** `RA_WATCH_TRIGGERS` (in `panel.js`, immediately after `RA_DIFF_TRACKED`) lists `(field, worseDir)` pairs; every `field` MUST also appear in `RA_DIFF_TRACKED` because the trigger reads `row._diff.<field>` populated by `_decorateRowsWithDiffs`. Adding a tracked field for the diff badge is the prerequisite — the watchlist trigger is then a one-line addition. Removing a field from `RA_DIFF_TRACKED` without first removing it from `RA_WATCH_TRIGGERS` would leave the trigger inert (silent — the `Math.sign` check on a missing diff falls through). The signed direction matters: `+1` = an increase is bad, `-1` = a decrease is bad. Don't add a field whose worsening direction is ambiguous.
- **Watchlist + filters interact.** Starred rows are STILL subject to `status` / `minScore` / `fleetFlyableOnly` filters. A user who stars a route then dials minScore above the route's score will see it disappear from the table — the star isn't a filter override in v1. Documented behaviour; surface as a setting (`watchlist.bypassFilters`) only if the user asks. Float-to-top operates on rows that survived filtering, so the visual contract is "starred routes float above the rest of *what you're already showing*."
- **Watchlist toggle is optimistic.** `_toggleWatchlist` flips the in-memory Set + re-renders BEFORE awaiting `RouteAssistantWatchlistStore.toggle()` to keep the UI snappy. On storage failure the in-memory state is reverted and the panel re-renders again. Don't reorder these — awaiting storage first would block the click on a slow disk.
- **Settings export envelope is a contract.** The exported JSON's `format` field MUST equal `AES_CONFIG_FORMAT` (currently `"aes-config"`); the importer refuses anything else. If you ever change the constant, importing files exported under the old name silently fails. Keep the constant stable; if a true breaking change to the envelope shape is needed, version it via the existing `version` field and add a migration shim in `_openImportConfig` rather than renaming `format`.
- **Settings export covers two namespaces only.** `settings.routeAssistant` and `settings.usedAircraftScanner`. Other top-level `settings.*` keys (e.g. station-automation state, schedule presets) are intentionally NOT exported — they're per-server runtime state, not user-tuned configuration. Adding a new exportable namespace requires editing `_exportConfig` (envelope build), `_openImportConfig` (diff inputs), and the apply branch in `_showImportDiffModal` (write-back). All three sites need to learn about the new namespace; missing one silently drops it from import.
- **Import write-back uses store-level save() per namespace.** `RouteAssistantSettings.save(block)` and `UsedAircraftPresets.save(block)` each do a top-level `Object.assign` against current+defaults — the imported block REPLACES the persisted top-level keys, but missing nested fields are deep-filled by the next `load()` from `_defaults()`. Don't bypass these stores and write directly to `chrome.storage.local["settings"]` — you'd lose the deep-merge.
- **Rating-observation log is append-only with FIFO cap 50 / 90d age prune.** `RouteAssistantRatingObservationStore.add` reads, prunes, appends, and trims in a single read-modify-write; concurrent writes from a bulk-sync are serialized by the scraper's concurrency=2 + stagger. Don't lift the cap past 50 without re-checking storage budgets across multi-hub users.
- **`_logRatingObservation` lives inside `ors-scraper.js`, NOT `panel.js`.** Bulk syncs call `scrape()` directly (via `bulkScrape` at line 809); logging in the panel would miss them. Don't move it.
- **Per-route per-class α resolution order in `_recomputeOrsSandbox` is `override > derived (≥minObs) > siblingDerived > fleetMedian > global default`.** Don't fork this order without auditing both the model's notes-footer source labels (`ors-model.js:project()` per-class push loop) and the override-expander's "auto: X" hint text — they read the same `alphaSourceByClass` map.
- **Sibling cascade is single-hop.** `siblingDerived` only fires from classes whose source on this route is already exactly `"derived"` (not `siblingDerived`/`fleetMedian`/`global`). Otherwise small biases would amplify across hops. Don't relax this without a stability test.
- **α stored everywhere as positive magnitude in `[0, 50]`.** The `_linregSlope` output is naturally negative; negate ONCE in `_ratingPriceElasticity` before clipping. Don't introduce a `Math.abs` at the consumer — that would mask sign bugs (a non-monotone regression that should reject would silently flip and pass).
- **Read α with `Number.isFinite`, never `||`.** A user override of `0` ("rating doesn't respond to price for this class on this route") is valid and must not fall through to the cascade. Already enforced at `ors-model.js` `_projectClass` and `panel.js` `_recomputeOrsSandbox`; new readers must follow the same pattern. The store's `_clean` also preserves `0` explicitly.
- **Auto-log default `true` is stable across versions.** Flipping default-on ↔ default-off silently changes user-observable behaviour (the observation log accumulates as a side effect of normal scrapes). If a future redesign requires the flip, version it via a one-time migration that preserves the user's last explicit choice.
- **`rating-alpha-store` is a separate sibling of `route-overrides-store` BY DESIGN.** Five other callers of `RouteAssistantRouteOverridesStore.save` exist; the existing `_clean` uses full-replace semantics that would silently erase α fields on every override save that doesn't pass them. Don't merge α back into the override store without first refactoring `_clean` to merge-with-existing semantics across every other caller.
- **Observation schema extensions are append-only.** Adding new fields (e.g. `seasonIndex`, `weekday` for slice 3 confounder controls) is fine; renaming or repurposing existing fields breaks the regression for any obs older than the rename. The store's `_isValidObservation` is the ground-truth admission gate — extend it (don't replace it) when adding new mandatory fields.
- **Confounder filters fire vs the prior SURVIVING obs, not the prior raw obs.** `_ratingPriceElasticity` walks observations sorted by `at`, maintains a `prior` reference that updates only when the current obs is kept. This means a chain of three consecutive comfort-changes drops only one (the one immediately after the change), not all three. Don't reset `prior` on drop — the regression would over-prune.
- **AFP form-driver NEVER POSTs and NEVER calls `submitBtn.click()`.** Slice D's `setOrigin` / `setDestination` / `setDepartureTime` / `setPricePercent` / `setService` / `fill` / `reverse` / `clear` / `dryRun` only mutate input values + dispatch `input`/`change` events; the user submits AS's own green Submit button. `dryRun(leg)` returns the would-be POST body for the "Show what'd post" UI — it does NOT send. Reverse O/D click IS allowed because that's a same-page UI toggle, not a form submission. New write affordances need separate review and a fresh invariant entry.
- **AFP Dashboard applier (`aircraft-flight-plan-dashboard/flight-number-applier.js`) is the SEPARATE module that will eventually POST — it does NOT change the form-driver invariant above.** Tier 1 ships dry-run only (constructor hard-codes `dryRunOnly:true` + `applyEnabled:false`; `apply()` always returns `{status:"dry-run", ...}` regardless of caller args). The applier never runs on the AFP page itself — it's only loaded via the `/app/fleets*` manifest entry, where it operates by proxying a GET against the aircraft's URL, parsing the form context, and (in T2+) POSTing back to the same Wicket form-action URL the GET returned. Tier 2 will introduce the real POST + verify + undo path under the same `dryRunOnly`+`applyEnabled` double-gate as `pricing-applier.js`. The two-gate model is non-negotiable here too — don't add a force-apply path that bypasses both. Audit log for every attempt lives in `aircraftFlightPlan:fnApplyLog` (global cap 200) + `aircraftFlightPlan:fnApplyLog:<server>:<aircraftId>` (per-aircraft cap 20), 5-min dedup on `(fingerprint+status)` mirrors `pricing-apply-log.js`.
- **AFP Dashboard manifest must NOT duplicate scripts already on `/app/fleets*` (extends the existing AFP de-dup invariant).** The `/app/fleets*` block already loads `state-store`, `active-draft-store`, `submit-bridge`, the four `schedule-management/*` files, and the fleet-hub stack. The dashboard expansion appends a 13-script dependency block (route-assistant settings/demand/score/distance/type-specs/overrides/notes/watchlist/wave-overlay/toast-host + flightsfrom data-store + aircraft-type-specs + AFP host/route-candidates/wave-applier) plus the 9 new dashboard files. AFP `host.js` is loaded here intentionally (it publishes `window.AesAfp.bus` for the route-candidates / wave-applier IIFE subscribers) and is safe because (a) it's idempotent — `if (window.AesAfp) return` at line 32, (b) `mount()` bails when `.as-page-aircraft` is missing, and (c) `content_aircraftFlightPlan.js` (which would call `mount()` on `window.load`) is NOT loaded on `/app/fleets*`. **Do not** add `form-driver.js` to this entry — it would render its toolbar into a missing slot and add unnecessary surface area. Verify with set-diff before any further manifest edit touching the dashboard block.
- **AFP slice files use defensive `typeof X !== "undefined"` guards on every cross-module dependency.** `RouteAssistantWatchlistStore`, `RouteAssistantToast`, `AESAircraftTypeSpecs`, `RouteAssistantWaveOverlay`, `SchedulePresets`, `FlightsFromStore`, etc. are all checked before use. This means the module degrades gracefully if a manifest regression drops a dependency — slices render their "waiting" state instead of throwing. Don't refactor to direct imports / hard `import` statements; the slice-isolation contract relies on type-of guards.
- **AFP manifest entry MUST NOT duplicate files already loaded via `/app/fleets*`.** `state-store.js` and the four `schedule-management/*` modules (range-buckets, presets-store, schedule-store, schedule-builder) are loaded by the broader `/app/fleets*` content_scripts entry, which also matches `/app/fleets/aircraft/*/0`. Listing the same file in both entries causes Chrome to inject it twice into the same isolated world; the second top-level `class X` execution throws `Identifier X has already been declared` and the page breaks. Verify with a Python set-diff (`afp_js & fleets_js` must be empty) before any manifest edit touching the AFP block.
- **AFP slice load order: foundation → host → bus subscribers → entry.** `settings-extension.js` (no bus dep) before `host.js` (publishes `window.AesAfp.bus`) before `audit-log.js` / `spec-resolver.js` / `route-candidates.js` / `form-driver.js` / `wave-applier.js` (all `bus.on(...)` at module-level IIFE load) before `content_aircraftFlightPlan.js` (calls `AesAfp.mount()` on `window.load`). Reordering would leave the bus subscribers calling against an undefined `window.AesAfp.bus`. The defensive guard at `audit-log.js:233` softens the failure to a warn but the slice silently disables itself — verify with `window.AesAfp.bus._handlers` in DevTools after mount.
- **AFP storage key prefix is `aircraftFlightPlan:`, namespaced separately from `routeAssistant:`.** Slice F's audit-log + state-store use this prefix to avoid collisions with RA stores. Don't merge into `routeAssistant:auditLog:*` — the AFP module is intended to be runnable independent of the RA panel (Fleet Hub reads `aircraftFlightPlan:state:*` without RA loaded). Cross-module reads use the `aircraftFlightPlan:` prefix as a stable contract.
- **AFP audit-log dual-store atomicity.** Every `add()` writes BOTH the global timeline (`aircraftFlightPlan:auditLog`, cap 200) AND the per-aircraft ring (`aircraftFlightPlan:auditLog:aircraft:<server>:<id>`, cap 50) in a single `chrome.storage.local.set({key1, key2})` call. Don't split the write — a partial failure would leave the two views diverged forever. The AesAfpAuditLog code path inside `add()` is the single source; never write directly to either key from outside.
- **AFP bus is the integration contract; slices DON'T import each other.** Slice D doesn't know about Slice C; it subscribes to `candidate:selected`. Slice F doesn't know about Slice D; it subscribes to `form:filled`. Adding direct module-to-module references (e.g. `AesAfpRouteCandidates.last` in form-driver) breaks the slice-isolation contract that makes partial-load + partial-failure recovery possible. New cross-slice features should emit/subscribe via `window.AesAfp.bus.emit(name, payload)` / `bus.on(name, fn)`.
- **AFP Tier 3 back-test logging is gated on `result.status` ∈ {`verified`, `posted`} — dry-run is excluded.** The back-fill loop (`_runBulkMarketScrape` → `backfillManyFromMarketShares`) doesn't distinguish trigger types; it back-fills any entry within the 8-day window with `observed.share == null`. Dry-run entries logged would get back-filled with a marketShare snapshot whose prices never actually changed — projecting the WRONG share against the SAME observed share, corrupting slice 3c's bias/RMSE metric. The gate at `panel.js renderResult` is the single point that enforces this; future tier3-apply log call sites must mirror it.
- **AFP Tier 3 back-test logging requires `sandboxProjected` to be present.** Only the sandbox-launched apply path (`_buildOrsSandboxScenarioCard` → "Apply this scenario as price…") sets `args.sandboxProjected`; manual row-context-menu applies don't. Skipping the gate would cause `RouteAssistantSandboxBacktestStore._normaliseEntry` to reject the entry (it requires `proj.share != null` OR `proj.paxPerWeek != null`), but the rejection is silent — an upstream check at the panel layer makes the gate explicit and avoids storage round-trips for entries that would just be dropped.
- **Toast cross-page continuity is intentional but bell stays panel-scoped.** `RouteAssistantToast.show()` mounts on every page that loads `toast-host.js` (now including AFP via the manifest expansion); the toast container is a singleton document-level overlay. The N2 notification bell popover is only mounted by `RouteAssistantPanel.init()` on the RA panel pages. AFP toasts flow through `RouteAssistantToast._history` (in-memory, ephemeral, capped at 50 via shift) and become visible via the bell on the user's NEXT visit to the RA panel. Closing the AFP tab clears the history slice (in-memory only). Don't lift the bell to the AFP page — outside-click + Escape teardown is local to the panel mount.

---

## 11 · What's been said (recent context)

- Auto-Pricing autonomy: c+d (one-click + batch-confirm) by default; silent-auto behind a separate explicit setting.
- Pricing UI: separate "Live route data" expander, not folded into Economics drawer.
- Tier order: 1 → 2a → 2b → 3 → 4. No skip-ahead.
